# Changelog

## 0.8.2

- U1a (items 3, 1, 2; ships with U1b, never alone): a key finding's verdict, example line and counts come from its own files. Item 3: TruffleHog's answers were matched to an ISR-S001/S002 by folder prefix, so the `~/.cursor/projects` group (its only hit a 64-hex line in `terminals/`) took the live GitHub answers of the `agent-tools` spill files and read "Github (8 live)" above an example line that wasn't GitHub. New `bound_keys` (fixplan.py): a finding takes the answers for its `affected_files` (every gitleaks group has them), else its own file, and for a file:line finding only that line (R06, as before); only a folder record with neither (an older version's) is still matched by folder. `key_status_severity`, `item_row` (the fix plan's reason) and `live_key_links` (now given the findings: only the live keys an open key finding stands for get a revoke link) all use it. `regroup_s002` keeps its own pre-filter to the kept files: without it the "every answer was 'didn't answer'" rule would be judged on every file's answers, not the kept ones (the plan said to drop it; kept on purpose). Item 1: one fingerprint helper, `secret_fp` (state.py: sha256 of the value, cut to 12 hex characters; decision D1, no per-run key), on each TruffleHog answer (from its Raw), each gitleaks member hit (the secret `gitleaks_hit` finds again in the file's bytes; `gitleaks_example` is now a wrapper of `_gitleaks_found` and `_example_why`, same signature) and each ISR-S001 hit (`secret_hits`; a key block's header gets none). The per-file ISR-S002 now has `member_hits` too (one file: the gate's answer is the same as for its path). Live, unsure and dead evidence reads `Github: 1 token, 16 copies, live, at a.txt:2, a.txt:4 and 14 more` (`key_evidence`): tokens are the distinct fingerprints among the bound answers, an answer without one takes the fingerprint of the finding's only secret on its line, else counts as a token of its own (never merged); copies are every place one of those tokens is, the answers' and the finding's own hits'. The evidence is rewritten after the key is made, so no key changes. Fingerprints are never stored: `state/key_status.json` is written without them and the gate drops them from every finding (`drop_fingerprints`, after the owner split); a snapshot keeps the counts in its evidence and the own-token mark. Item 2: `own_gh_token()` runs `gh auth token` (no API call) once per audit, only when the first secret is found, and keeps only its fingerprint, in memory (`OWN_TOKEN`); a found secret with that fingerprint is marked `own_token: "gh"` when it is found, and its finding says "- the same token as this computer's own gh sign-in", with the fix "This is the GitHub token gh and git push use on this computer. Make a new one at github.com/settings/tokens, sign gh in with it, revoke this one..." (`OWN_FIX`; `plain()` picks `OWN_TOKEN_PLAIN` first). Severity, title and label are unchanged. Tests: new `tests/test_v082_secrets.py`, 10 tests: 7 replays (U1a reports them failing on e9184df at their assertion; U1fix1 did not re-run that): the `.cursor/projects` group is KEY_LIVE; "Generic (1 live)" names no place; a Github revoke link from the spill file; "Github (8 live)"; "Github (2 live)"; no own-token note on ISR-S002; none on ISR-S001; and 3 that pass there too: a group's own live key is still live (control), another token is not the own one (control), no fingerprint is stored (guard). `tests/test_boxes.py` Box.setUp patches `isr.own_gh_token` to return None (one line; tests never run gh). No assertion changed. docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md "v0.8.2" and skills/isr-bot-fix say what changed.
- U1b (items 5, 4; ships with U1a): new ISR-S006 "Tool output holds a whole environment dump", high (decision D3). The real leak U1a's item 3 stopped borrowing a verdict for: Cursor's bash tool wrote three agent-tools spill files, each a dump of the whole shell environment, with the box's own gh token on 16 lines and its CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN on 14; tool output is silent (0.8.1) and two files were over ISR-S001's 300,000-byte read limit. Audit step 4 reads a spill file (a `.txt` directly in a folder named `agent-tools` or `terminals`: `spill_place`) up to 20,000,000 bytes (`SPILL_READ_LIMIT`, the fix plan's limit); the key and decoy-copy checks read it as before, only up to 300,000 bytes. `spill_dump` (secrets_pkgs.py) calls a file a dump when its `NAME=value` lines (`export `, `declare -x ` or xtrace's `+ ` allowed) have at least 15 different names, 3 of them the shell's own, and reports only real secrets in it: a SCRUB key shape anywhere in the file, or a TOKEN/SECRET/PASSWORD/PASSWD/API_KEY/PRIVATE_KEY/CREDENTIAL variable's value (16+ characters, not a path), each only when `secret_kind` calls it real (no placeholder, no `$VAR`). `spill_findings` makes one ISR-S006 per folder: evidence `3 files, 2 secrets (CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN, GH_TOKEN - this computer's own gh token), 30 copies` (names and counts by U1a's fingerprints, never a value or a fingerprint), `affected_files`, key from the folder (`ident`), `own_token: "gh"` from U1a's `own_token_of`, and fix "Delete these files ... rotate each named secret ... stop it" plus how to rotate the own gh token (`SPILL_FIX`, `SPILL_OWN_FIX`; `plain()` picks `SPILL_OWN_PLAIN` for it). `owner_of_finding`: ISR-S006 in tool output (fixture-echo) is the owner's; in a vendor folder or ISR Bot's own trees it is as its files are; the echo ISR-S001/S002 for the same files stay silent. ISR-S006 joins EVIDENCE_RULES and PLACE_WHOLE_RULES (scoring.py) and the brief's "Secrets on the shared computer". Item 4: gitleaks gets rule `anthropic-oauth-token` (`\b(sk-ant-oat\d{2}-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{40,})`, entropy 3.5, keyword `sk-ant-oat`) before the first `[[allowlists]]`; the entropy floor keeps `sk-ant-oat01-xxxx...` placeholders out (no name-based skip). No API call checks any token. Tests: new `tests/test_v082_spill.py`, 15 tests: 8 replays (no S006 for the v0.8.1 spill files, a terminals file, `/workspace/agent-tools`, a TOKEN-named value, an oat-only dump; no gitleaks rule; the real gitleaks finds no oat token; a value or fingerprint stored is checked once S006 exists), shown failing by ablation only: edaca62 run with the `spill_findings` call and the gitleaks rule taken out gave 8 failures at their assertions; they were not run on the real bases (ca18f8a, e9184df), so a failure there is not established by a run; 7 controls that pass there too (a build log with 5 assignments, a 10-name `env` listing, a 30-variable config with no shell names, a dump of placeholders and `$VARS`, a dump in `ws/projects/app/notes.txt`, a printenv and an oat token in `isr-bot-dev`). No existing assertion changed. docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md "v0.8.2" (thresholds, D2 as decided, the oat rule's limits) and skills/isr-bot-fix say what changed.
- U1fix1 (Codex's review of U1a+U1b, approved with fixes). (1) The own-token fix is added to a key finding's fix, never in place of it: `mark_own` appends `OWN_FIX` (now "The token marked as this computer's own gh sign-in is the one gh and git push use: make a new one at github.com/settings/tokens, sign gh in with it, then revoke the old one."; `with_own`), `plain()` keeps the finding's own title and how and appends it (`OWN_TOKEN_PLAIN` is gone), and `needs_you` appends it after naming the providers, so a group holding the own gh token and another live key keeps "Turn these off at the provider: Github, Slack" and both links. ISR-S006 is unchanged (`SPILL_OWN_PLAIN`). (2) `key_evidence`: a TruffleHog answer without a fingerprint is always a token of its own (`k.get("fp") or ("answer", i)`); the location map that let it take the fingerprint of the only secret on its line is gone (docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md updated). (3) `spill_dump`: SCRUB's AWS secret shape starts at its name (`AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY=`), so an unquoted assignment counted as two secrets ("2 secrets (AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY, 1 with no name), 2 copies"); its 40-character value is now the secret, tied to its assignment, and the name rule skips it (`AWS_SECRET_SHAPE`). (4) `item_row`: a key finding whose evidence has key_evidence's "N tokens, M copies" (`KEY_COUNTS`) shows that evidence (places and own-token note included) instead of "Github: 8 live"; an older record without it keeps the per-status reason. (5) The fix preview's `only_you` entry for ISR-S006 has `dumps`: each dump's names-only evidence and its files (`item_row` gives an S006 row `files`, whole paths). (6) skills/isr-bot-fix: the cleanup deletes only spill files under a `safe_fixes` path; the others (a terminals file, an agent-tools folder that changed in the last day) are named for the user to delete by hand; nothing deletes more than before. (7) The U1a and U1b lines above: replay counts corrected (U1a: 7 replays and 3 controls/guards, not 8 and 2) and U1b's base failures marked as an ablation result. Tests: 6 new, all run on edaca62 itself (the real base for this unit): 5 replays fail there at their assertion (tests/test_v082_secrets.py U1Fix1: the mixed group's fix; answers without a fingerprint; the fix plan row; tests/test_v082_spill.py U1Fix1: the unquoted AWS secret; the fix preview's dump entry) and 1 control passes there (an older record's row keeps "Github: 8 live"). No existing assertion changed; tests/test_v082_spill.py's docstring now says how its replays were shown.
- U2 (item 6): a reviewed skill whose only change is the product's former name now written "ISR Bot" is no longer a HIGH "Reviewed skill or plugin changed". New `rename_only(p, h, base)` (approved.py): the file is name-only when (a) the skill's approved copy has its text and the baseline's digest for it, (b) the masked copy and the masked file now have the same lines, (c) each line that differs differs only by that one word swapped for "ISR Bot" (word tokens walked side by side, `_name_swaps`), in prose (`_prose_at`: no slash, backslash, underscore, hyphen, dot, backtick, @, $ or colon touching it, except `.`/`:`/`,` before a space or the line's end), and (d) the bytes read now (the audit's own digest) with every swap undone hash to the approved digest, so a change inside a masked value, or anywhere else, keeps it HIGH. The word is held as `FORMER_NAME_SHA256` only. audit.py: such a file is ISR-I001 "Reviewed skill changed: product name only", low, evidence "<former name> → ISR Bot on N lines" (a new key: the HIGH line reads as fixed once and this one as new), `rename_only`, and `diff` (at most 6 masked -/+ line pairs, 120 characters each), which `compact` passes on (audit `new`, daily, the delta file). Low, so it is never grouped with the HIGH changes and never makes "Check this week". scoring.py `corroborated`: a name-only ISR-I001 backs up nothing. New `fix --rename-reviewed <skill>[,<skill>] --owner-said-yes` (`rename_reviewed`, isr.py argument): each open name-only file of a named skill is checked again on bytes read once; those still name-only get their digest in the baseline and their text in the approved copy (made from the same bytes; every other file keeps its approved entry, so another change stays open); ledger event `reapprove_rename`; refused without `--owner-said-yes`, for a skill with no open name-only change (so never a platform file or ISR Bot's own), and while the baseline is reset. The fix preview's `changed_skills` row says `rename_only` (true when every changed file in it is) and gives the command in `rename_reviewed`. Deviation from the plan: the token diff walks both lines side by side (a former-name token against "ISR", " ", "Bot") instead of difflib.SequenceMatcher, which can pair the spaces of a line with two swaps the wrong way; (d) decides either way. The approved copy is written by `save_approved` directly (not `save_approved_all`, whose no-held-file branch reads the files again). Tests: new `tests/test_v082_rename_only.py`, 9 tests: 7 replays and the 7 HIGH controls of `test_anything_else_stays_high` (an added line, the name in a path, in a code span, another word swapped, a changed value inside a masked URL on another line, no approved copy, another name), plus a guard that the digest is the name table's. The replays were shown failing at their assertion by ablation, not by a run on d928eb7: audit.py's `rename_only` call taken out failed all 7 and the name-only skill beside the controls (high instead of low; the pipe-to-shell line Confirmed instead of Worth a look; the preview row without `rename_only`), scoring.py's test taken out failed the "backs up nothing" one, `compact`'s diff taken out failed the "low with its diff" one, the preview row taken out failed the preview one; the 7 controls passed under each. No existing assertion changed. docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md "v0.8.2: name-only skill changes" and skills/isr-bot-fix (`--rename-reviewed`) say what changed.
- U3 (item 7): one engine is not "Confirmed", a documents-only skill's lone SkillSpector flag is medium, and a plugin cache's copy of the owner's skill is counted with it. The v0.8.1 HIGH on make-bot-ui (one SKILL.md; SkillSpector "Supply Chain: External Script Fetching", "Tool Misuse: Chaining Abuse", which name no kind of risk; husk clean) read Confirmed because ISR Bot's own pattern hit on its installer line, another source in the same folder, "backed up" the folder verdict by the v0.6.8 no-kind rule. scoring.py `corroborated`: an ISR-X001 or ISR-X002 is backed up only by a second engine's finding (source skillspector, husk or engines, `ENGINE_SOURCES`, not its own) on a place it covers or that covers it, or by an ISR-I004 (a new plugin); ISR Bot's own hits, evidence and drift no longer do (when ISR Bot's high hit names the engine's kind, engine_findings already makes ISR-X003). Deviation from the plan: the plan's one-liner kept only ISR-I004; a second engine's verdict is kept too, so two engines agreeing still confirm (tests/test_v069.py:51 pins it). engines.py: a lone ISR-X001 on the owner's skill is medium instead of high when `doc_only_skill` (every file at any depth `.md`/`.markdown`/`.txt`/`.rst`, no link, at most ENGINE_MAX_FILES; narrower than the plan's "every file skill_files lists": skipped folders and links count) and SkillSpector's kinds name none of `DOC_ONLY_KEEP_HIGH` (credential, exfil, secrecy, override, obfuscation). ownership.py `fold_plugin_copies`, called by `owner_gate` after the split: a vendor ISR-X001/X002/X003 in a plugin cache (`OWNER_PLUGIN_CACHE`; narrower than the plan's every vendor record, so the "plugin-cache copies" wording is true) with the same rule and skill name as an owner finding goes into its `also_at`, the evidence gets "· also in N plugin-cache copies" (replaced, never repeated; the key is the one finding() made), and it leaves the vendor list (one less in the platform notes count and `breakdown`). Tests: new `tests/test_v082_engines.py`, 10 tests: 4 replays and 6 controls. Replays, shown failing at their assertion by ablation (the three changes undone together, which is e9184df's code for these parts; not run on bb7d591 or e9184df): the make-bot-ui shape is `("corroborated", "Confirmed", "high")` instead of Worth a look; the same as a unit (Confirmed by an ISR-T006 in the folder); a documents-only skill is high instead of medium; the plugin cache's copy is a vendor record of its own. Controls, passing under the ablation too: two engines (ISR-X003 critical, and an ISR-X001 + ISR-X002 pair) confirm; an ISR-X001 in a new plugin stays Confirmed medium; a `run.sh`, a `config.json`, a link or a `node_modules/x.js` keeps it high; a "Credential Theft" reason keeps it high; another skill name in the cache and the same name in `.grok/bundled` stay vendor records. No existing assertion changed (none pinned an ISR-X001 Confirmed through ISR Bot's own hits; the regression modules the plan names were read, not run, per the unit's test rules). docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md "v0.8.2: one engine alone, documents-only skills, plugin-cache copies" says what changed.
- U4 (items 8, 9): ISR-T014 needs a listener, and the score says which way is better. Item 8: the v0.8.1 MEDIUM on link-prospecting/SKILL.md:57 ("Only record contact details that were actually found. Include the source URL for any email, profile, or contact form.") matched "any email" and read Confirmed because "Send at most 10 queries per call." in the same file is an ISR-T013, a wording backer (scoring.py `corroborated`, WORDING_RULES branch). The two other hits on the box were checked from their lines (PLAN.md item 8): an email course's SKILL.md:76 "each email" is a banner layout note, an outreach skill's references/email.md:19 "every email touch" says where a signature goes; both false positives. text.py `scan_text`: an ISR-T014 match counts only when `listener` says so: "new"/"incoming" in the match (`LISTENER_NEW`) or a listener word in its sentence (`LISTENER_WORDS`, `sentence_of`, next to the ISR-T016 filter). The regex, evidence and key of every match that still counts are unchanged; WORDING_BACKERS is unchanged (T013 still confirms a real listener that sends). Deviation from the plan: `inbound` and `coming in` added to the plan's listener words (they only keep more matches). Item 9: verified in scoring.py `score` (100 minus the penalty; the grade is then capped by the status). New `SCORE_MEANING` (scoring.py), shown as a "Score:" line after "Status:" in the report (render_md), under "Posture score" on the dashboard (render_html) and in the brief's "Security score" metric (isr.py render_brief); not in the audit/daily JSON. skills/isr-bot-audit and isr-bot-report say "higher is better". Tests: new `tests/test_v082_wording.py`, 9 tests: 5 replays and 4 controls. Replays, shown failing at their assertion with this unit's product changes undone (the T014 filter and the three SCORE_MEANING lines; not run on 473ffa7 or e9184df): the real L57 line with the T013 line gives a Confirmed medium ISR-T014 (by scan_text + label_findings, and by a full audit on a box); the layout and signature lines give ISR-T014; the report, dashboard and brief lack the sentence. Controls: "every new message", "whenever any email arrives", "all messages in the support inbox", "as soon as each notification comes in", "watch ... every event" keep ISR-T014 with the same evidence and severity; a listener next to a send stays Confirmed; the silent-sender and routine-bad fixtures keep ISR-T014; the score is 100 with nothing and lower with a finding. Changed existing test input (not an assertion): tests/test_item8_dryrun.py:73 "For every email, open with the hook." -> "For every new email that comes in, open with the hook." (the old line no longer matches, so `assertTrue(t14)` would fail; the plan's "Whenever a new email comes in" has no every/each/any/all and would not match ISR-T014 either); its three assertions are unchanged. docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md "v0.8.2: broad listener wording (ISR-T014) and the score's direction".
- U5 (item 10): test_dq013 no longer depends on the box's speed (tests/test_boxes.py only, no product code). The base test had 2-second launch caps for real launches of the fake scanners, so on a busy box a healthy launch was marked stuck, a 14-second budget for two real batches, and a 40-second timer on the whole second audit. Now (`MainBoxLessons.dq013_box`): a jam answers at once with exit 124, which skillspector_batch reads as a time-out the way it reads a killed scanner (the real kill stays covered by `test_one_skill_that_hangs_the_scanner_costs_one_launch_not_the_run`, which keeps JAM_SS); budgets 600 and caps of 60 seconds; the fake logs the skills each launch was given, and the second audit is checked launch by launch: no launch holding a changed skill comes at or after the first launch holding a stuck one, and each stuck one is retried alone. Deviation from the plan: its second-run solo limit of 60 would not make the stuck entries due (a first-run solo cap is max(BASE 60, SOLO 30) = 60, kept as the entry's limit, and an entry is retried only when its limit is below the solo limit), so the second run uses SOLO 120. Changed assertion (decision D4): `self.assertLess(time.monotonic() - t0, 40)` -> `self.assertEqual([l for l in launches[first_jam:] if fresh_n & set(l)], [], launches)` and `self.assertEqual(sorted(l for l in launches if jam_n & set(l)), sorted([x] for x in jam_n))`. Kept unchanged: the stuck == jams assertion after the first audit and the "all 28 scanned" one. New `test_dq013_order_holds_on_a_slow_box` (8 skills, 2 jams, 0.6 seconds per launch). Replay, both runs by a temporary edit, undone: the base test with a 2.5-second sleep in every fake launch failed at its stuck == jams assertion (all 34 skills stuck instead of the 6 jams; 72.8 s); the new test with the same sleep passed (112.2 s). Mutation check, undone: engines.py's DQ-013 retry list emptied (stuck skills back in the normal batches, as in v0.6.8) fails both new tests at the launch-order assertion.

## 0.8.1

- Release 0.8.1, ownership by location (the owner's decisions, Oct 24 and Oct 25). ISR Bot decides who owns a file by where it is, never by a list of files or hashes: one function, `owner_of(path)` (new `isr_bot/isr_parts/ownership.py`), checks path prefixes on the path as written and on its real path, and one gate in the audit sorts every finding before scoring. Grok Bot's, Codex's and Claude's own folders (`managed-skills` and `plugins/cache` under `sand-data` and `agent-data`, `.claude/plugins`, `.codex/skills`, `.codex/plugins`, `.grok/bundled`, `/tmp/hooks`) are the vendor's: never scored, one number on the platform notes line (`platform_notes.count`; the records are kept in the snapshot's `vendor` list), and `isr.py breakdown` lists every one. ISR Bot's own dev and test trees, `/workspace/backups` and copies of chat and tool output (`.claude/projects`, `.claude/plans`, the `agent-tools` folders) are silent. Everything else is the owner's and is scored as in 0.8.0. A path is not the owner's only when the path as written and its real path are in the same class, so a link either way between the owner's files and those folders stays the owner's: an owner path linked into a vendor folder stays a counted high, a platform folder replaced by a link to outside every platform folder is not built-in and stays a counted alert, and a small link-only walk of vendor folders keeps the files behind a link out of one scored (the owner's decisions, Oct 25). Integrity tracking (fingerprints, `baseline.json`, approved copies) covers only the owner's files; a 0.8.0 baseline's vendor lines are ignored on read and gone at the next save, so a vendor update raises nothing. Every reader of `state/last_findings.json` but `events clear` (a mutation-only reader) goes through `load_snapshot()`, so a 0.8.0 snapshot shows no vendor, self or echo finding as the owner's, and its score, grade and status are recomputed from what it keeps. gitleaks's grouped ISR-S002 and OSV's ISR-D002 are split by member file or project, with their keys rebuilt from the kept members, so a backup copy or an echo coming and going gives no new/fixed pair. vet splits files by owner before it scans, stages or joins them: score and verdict come from the owner's files only, and with none it says "Platform file (never scored)" or "Not scored: ISR Bot's own files or tool output". rules/platform-release.lock is no longer read: it, `rules/platform-reviewed.json` and `rules/platform-normalize.json` moved (bytes unchanged) to `tests/fixtures/platform_v080/`, where only the parser tests read them. Deleted: the 0.8.0 platform pipeline (the release-list and reviewed-list checks, the b8i4 middle path and its Platform refresh line, `platform_unverified` in daily and fix, the platform walk and its ISR-I011, `state/platform_held.json`, the alias branch, the catalog-plugin rule, vet's release-hash discount). `fix --approve-platform` and `--platform-from` stay in the parser and are refused: "NOT APPROVED: Platform files are never scored, so there is nothing to approve." Known limits (docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md "v0.8.1: ownership by location", THREAT_MODEL.md "Known gaps"): a tampered platform file goes unseen (it is only counted); a key in a chat transcript, a tool cache, ISR Bot's dev tree or `/workspace/backups` is not seen; plugins the owner installs are not scored; `fix --revet` can't re-approve the owner's file that a link inside a platform folder leads to, and `baseline --owner-said-yes` clears it (the owner's decision, Oct 25: left as is). VERSION 0.8.1, in README's install line, the setup skill (lines 13, 19, 22) and install.sh (lines 3, 11); `bot/getting-started.md`'s skills link still says `tree/v0.8.0/skills` (template text, not changed in this build). README, THREAT_MODEL.md, docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md (the b8i4 section is headed "Retired in 0.8.1 (history)"), skills/isr-bot-audit, skills/isr-bot-fix and routines/daily-watch.md say what changed. Tests: new `tests/test_v081_ownership.py`, 69 tests; existing tests converted to the new rule, none deleted; README still says "more than 2000 tests".

## 0.8.0

- Release 0.8.0, the full rename: everything is named ISR Bot (intelligence, surveillance, reconnaissance), not only the display text 0.7.4 changed. The engine is `isr_bot/isr.py` with its parts in `isr_bot/isr_parts/`; the rule IDs are `ISR-*`; every setting the engine reads is an `ISR_BOT_*` variable (the scripts' own are `ISR_*`, such as `ISR_HOME`); the install folder is `/workspace/isr-bot` and the repo `grokbot-isr-bot`; the skill and routine slugs are `isr-bot-*` (`vet-template` is unchanged); the JSON keys, the engine labels (`ISR Bot rules`), the audit stage (`ISR Bot's own tools`), the evidence texts that are part of a finding key, the User-Agent (`ISR-Bot/<version>`) and the gitleaks config's description all use the new name. Nothing keeps the old one, and nothing reads it. Clean start, no upgrade path from 0.7.x: 0.8.0 doesn't read an older install's folder, settings or state, and it no longer recognises the old routine names or an old saved getting-started skill. Install it fresh in `/workspace/isr-bot` with the setup skill; routines an older template set up keep their old names until the template is updated, and uninstall names only the new ones. Rule IDs and evidence are part of every finding key, so on the first 0.8.0 run every finding is new: earlier accepts and dismissals don't carry over, so re-dismiss by hand (docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md "v0.8.0: the full rename (clean start, no upgrade path)"). Also in this release, the reword planned for 0.7.5: "Anything you scan" and "Anything you are vetting" in place of "Content you scan" and "Content you are vetting" (bot/description.md, memory 6, the vet-template skill). VERSION 0.8.0, in README's install line, the setup skill (lines 13, 19, 22), install.sh (lines 3, 11) and getting-started's skills link (`tree/v0.8.0/skills`). Tests: `tests/test_v080_leftovers.py` fails on the old name in any tracked file's content or path outside the places that keep it on purpose (this file from 0.7.4 down, `tests/golden_expect/*.json`, docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md below "Before 0.8.0", and the name table `tests/golden_rename_v080.json`); a test that needs an old spelling reads it from that table through `tests/rename_v080.py`. No test deleted or weakened. README now says "more than 2000 tests" (2055 tests at 0.8.0).

## 0.7.4

- Release 0.7.4, the display name (owner decisions, Oct 22 and Oct 23): the product is called ISR Bot (intelligence, surveillance, reconnaissance) in display text only. Every message, report, page, finding title and fix the engine prints says ISR Bot (the report's `# ISR Bot report`, the dashboard and threat-brief titles and footers, SELF-CHECK FAILED, BUSY, the "ISR Bot, fix it" prompt, the roll-call question, PP09's title, the fix plan's `owner`), and so do the skills' prose and descriptions, the routines, the routine names in `routines/descriptions.json`, `rules/feeds.json`'s release name, install.sh's and publish.sh's messages, and README's title and text (with "(the engine and its files keep the name Watchtower)"); THREAT_MODEL.md, EGRESS.md, SECURITY.md and docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md got new titles or intros only. `fixplan.REDACTED` is "[removed by ISR Bot]". Kept on purpose, listed in docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md "v0.7.4" and in the new guard test's ALLOWED: the paths, the repo, `WATCHTOWER_*`, `wt.py`, the WT-* IDs, the `watchtower-*` slugs, state and report file names, JSON keys and code identifiers; the engine labels `Watchtower rules` and `Watchtower` (engines_used.json, the score fingerprint and the WT-X003 agreement evidence: renaming them would print a false SCANNERS MISSING after the upgrade and bring every reported, accepted or dismissed WT-X003 back as new); two evidence texts that are part of a finding key (WT-K005's "during a recorded Watchtower run" and the WT-A003 language Tip's); the User-Agent `Watchtower/<version>`; the gitleaks config's description. Old names still recognised: uninstall's preview names the new routine names and, for routines set up before 0.7.4, the old ones (`LEGACY_ROUTINE_NAMES`); a saved getting-started copy that says "You are Watchtower" or "You are ISR Bot" and differs from the release is reported as older (so every saved copy shows as stale until it is re-saved: intended); a brief's update named Watchtower or ISR Bot gives the update note; prepublish doesn't count the name ISR Bot as a roster name (WT-PP08) in an unchanged copy of this install's own template files only (fix round 1). Template prep (the template itself is not updated): new `bot/card.json` (name and card description), memory 3 says ISR Bot, new memories 9 (SELF-CHECK FAILED) and 10 (the name, the kept folder names, cost, roll-call off), all under 480 characters; bot/description.md stays at or under 2200 characters (2176: "your own files changed", "Run your scripts"); bot/getting-started.md says "You are ISR Bot" and gains its own paragraph pointing to the Skills tab and the v0.7.4 skills folder on GitHub. VERSION 0.7.4, in README's install line, the setup skill (lines 13, 19, 22) and install.sh (lines 3, 11). Golden: a new expect kind in `tests/golden_split.py`, `subst` {"pairs": [["Watchtower", "ISR Bot"]]}, which takes an item whole only when pre and post both exist and differ, pre holds no "ISR Bot" and post with the name put back is pre byte for byte; any other pair is refused, so it is no general rewrite rule; `tests/golden_expect/v074.json`, item `v074`. Tests: `tests/test_v074_display_name.py` (engine strings, prose, the name never shortened, the card and memories, and replays for prepublish, SAFE_CLAIM, the update note, the saved getting-started copy and uninstall) and a `SubstKind` class in `tests/test_golden_expect.py`. Changed existing assertions, display text only (Watchtower -> ISR Bot in the expected text): test_watchtower, test_batch3_selfcheck_docs, test_batch3_final_review, test_batch3_claude_review2, test_batch3_scanners, test_batch3_fix_round1, test_split_guard (D3), test_hardening_batch1, test_batch5_access_map, test_batch5_access_round1/2/3, test_batch5_brief_access (and its TEMPLATE_WORDS: the new name's words for "Watchtower"), test_batch6_failed_lookups, test_batch6_connectors, test_batch5_platform_notes, test_batch5_codex_r1, test_batch5_codex_r3, test_batch5_platform_normalize, test_batch6_ask_first_i18n, test_batch6_platform_verify, test_batch6_release_files, test_generic_release (the policy line and the four routine names) and test_b8i4_fix_round1 (PLATFORM_MOVED's wording). Forced, not display text: `test_watchtower.py::test_template_memories_fit_the_platform_limit` expects 10 memories (was 8) and checks memories 3, 9 and 10 exactly; `test_scripts.py`'s two bump tests use a computed `next_version()` (VERSION's patch plus one) instead of the hardcoded 0.7.4, which is no bump once VERSION is 0.7.4. No test deleted or weakened. Fix round 1 (Codex review of cffd4b7, 3 medium and 3 low): (MED1) the audit stage keeps its stored name `Watchtower's own tools` (stage_times.json, progress.json, stages_skipped), and `STAGE_LABELS`/`stage_label` show it as ISR Bot's own tools in the FAILED note, the report's "Skipped this run", the brief and daily's NO_CHANGES_BUT_NOT_CHECKED line; (MED2) `subst` refuses an item in which any kept string (`SUBST_KEEP` in golden_split.py: the engine labels, the User-Agent token, that stage key, the kept evidence texts, the legacy routine names and getting-started marker, the gitleaks description) is seen a different number of times in post than in pre, and v074.json's catch-all subst entry takes only commands' stdout and stderr and reports/*.md|html; (MED3) prepublish's global skip of the new name (in `known_names`) and SAFE_CLAIM's pattern for the new name are taken out again (back to accbe5e): the name is exempt from WT-PP08 only in an unchanged copy of this install's own template files (`own_release_texts`), and SAFE_CLAIM knows only "watchtower" (both open owner decisions then; SAFE_CLAIM's still is, docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md); (LOW4) three finding titles reworded so the whole name is within breakdown's 48-character column (the platform metadata title, the "on ISR Bot's list" WT-I002, the WT-A003 language Tip; WT-PP09's, reworded then too, has its old wording again with the shorter name: "Getting-started is older than this ISR Bot"); (LOW5) the name is never written without "Bot" (a new repo-wide guard); (LOW6) the rest of THREAT_MODEL.md's and EGRESS.md's operational prose says ISR Bot. Fix round 2 (Codex verify of d7758f6, 2 medium): (MED2) `subst_change` is field-aware: in JSON (a document that starts a line, one per line or pretty-printed, or a `"key": value` line of one that didn't parse) the rename may change only the string values of the display fields in `SUBST_FIELDS` (title, fix, notes, lines, more, platform, next, warning, what, how, why, owner); any other field, any object key or a JSON string in no list refuses the item, and `SUBST_KEEP_PROSE`'s WT-X005 exception holds only in a `fix` value or plain text; reports/*.md|html stay plain text (`SUBST_PLAIN_KEY`); (MED3) prepublish's exemption is bound to the checked release: one read of each file, and the name is exempt only when the sha256 of those raw bytes is one that the self-checked MANIFEST.sha256 (`release_manifest`) lists for bot/*, routines/* or skills/*/SKILL.md (`own_release_digests`, `read_once`; no folder listing, no text compare); the owner decided (Oct 23) to keep exactly this, for the name ISR Bot only. Tests: 8 more in `SubstKind`, 4 more in `test_v074_display_name.py`'s `Prepublish`; README says "more than 1850 tests" (2008). The name, second decision (owner, Oct 23): the display name is ISR Bot everywhere the rename above put the first 0.7.4 name; everything kept on purpose is unchanged. Rewords revisited: the threat brief's "Paste this into your ISR Bot"; uninstall's "Delete ISR Bot itself"; WT-PP09's title is "Getting-started is older than this ISR Bot" again (its old wording fits now); fix --help's --quarantine text says "out of use and into ISR Bot's quarantine folder" (the comma is gone: at argparse's 54-column help width the first line ended with the name's first word with or without it, so the name split; "and" moves the whole name to the next line); the WT-A003 Tip and the two platform titles keep fix round 1's wording (the old ones put the name past column 48 or after a part of unknown length). The LOW5 guard is now: the first name is gone from every tracked file (v074.json's `block` entries aside until the next golden run regenerates them), and the name is always "ISR Bot" (never its first word alone, no doubled Bot). Prepublish: the WT-PP08 exemption stays as fix round 2 made it, for the name ISR Bot only, in any case (the owner's decision); a new test changes one byte of an own file and it is flagged. SAFE_CLAIM's expected failure is retargeted to the new name. Golden: `SUBST_PAIRS` is [["Watchtower", "ISR Bot"]]; v074.json's about, why and subst values and v074-version.json's entries say ISR Bot. Tests: the name swapped in every test that had it, `test_an_update_named_isr_bot` renamed, one new `Prepublish` test.
- b8i4, platform middle path (owner decision, Oct 22; closes the item 8 / DQ-042 gap: every platform update was one counted high per file, on every box, every day). A new or changed file in a platform folder that `rules/platform-release.lock` doesn't list with these bytes is now a candidate when its path already exists (a release-list line with any hash, or a `baseline.json` line) or it is in a new platform skill folder, and it is strictly inside a platform root as written and as its real path, is no link itself, has no colon in its path and isn't under a folder the platform walk couldn't finish, while the release list is usable. After the scanners, a candidate is held (the same counted high as before, its fix starting with why) when Watchtower's rules hit at medium or above in exactly the bytes it fingerprinted (guarded records included), when it is over 1 MB, not plain text or unreadable, or when a WT-X003 on its folder isn't a wording-only platform vote (a dangerous kind, or an answer that may lack one). A new skill folder is all or nothing; a new file in a known skill folder is quiet only beside a clean update of that folder in the same run. Anything else is the quiet "Platform refresh" line and rolls into the baseline on a full audit (a wording-only vote no longer holds a middle-path update back; a listed update still waits for it). A first run is silent for a clean candidate and leaves it out of the first baseline; in daily, a scanner line kept from the last run holds a quiet update in its folder until the next full audit. The Platform refresh line counts each vendor (`2 of Grok Bot's own files changed, 1 of Codex's`), says when the middle path let files through, lists every file it covers (`files`) and as many paths as fit in its evidence. The same managed skill under `sand-data/managed-skills` and `agent-data/managed-skills` (same sha256, same rule) is one finding naming the other place (`also_at`). Watchtower's own released skill copied into a platform folder is never exempted as Watchtower's own. `fix --approve-platform` stays refused, with new wording (the owner's yes never approves a platform file; a file Watchtower's checks pass is accepted on its own); `baseline --owner-said-yes` still holds every unlisted platform file. The lock: the 33 lines for the maintainer's plugin sync bucket are gone, 13 come back as `.claude/plugins/synced/<bucket>/…` lines with the same hashes (the vendor's own cowork-plugin-management and the bucket's 3 loader files); the 20 lines of a third-party plugin are dropped; `<bucket>` is the only placeholder allowed (any other makes the list unusable); 604 entries; no personal names in its header. `tests/test_generic_release.py` denies the old bucket ids exactly (21 digests) and no longer exempts `tests/test_item8_churn.py`. THREAT_MODEL.md, docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md (b8i4 section), README.md (and "more than 1800 tests"), routines/daily-watch.md and skills/watchtower-fix/SKILL.md say what changed. Tests: `tests/test_b8i4_middle_path.py`, new placeholder tests in `tests/test_item8_churn.py`, and the item 8, batch 5 and batch 6 tests that encoded the Oct 18 rule now expect the quiet line (their plants are loose files where the point was a plant). Fix round 1 (Codex review of f9c3d72, 6 high and 3 medium, all fixed): (1) a new skill folder, or a new file in one, is a candidate only in an update run (`platform_update_candidates(..., established, held_before)`), and a file the last run held outside the middle path (an unverified finding without the new `platform_candidate` mark, which every middle-path hold now carries) is never one (`platform_held_before`, read from `state/last_findings.json`), so a first run's new folder is a counted high on that run and every run after; an "update" of a folder needs a file the baseline has. (2) A candidate's real path must be inside a platform folder as the home folder really has it (`platform_real_roots`), so a platform folder replaced by a link to an outside folder gets no middle path. (3) `platform_listed` and `platform_lock_path` refuse a file that is itself a link. (4) `settle_platform_updates` reads each candidate again (exactly the fingerprinted bytes), runs the whole rule set on it (`platform_full_verdict`: a SKILL.md as a skill, the rest as reference files, DQ-024's template exception kept; a metadata JSON file's text values as skill text, so WT-T013 applies, and one that isn't JSON is held) and holds it on any other medium-or-higher finding of the run on the file (`_flagged_here`: WT-M001, WT-S001, WT-S002 and the rest); daily holds on one kept from the last run (`hold_on_kept_votes`); audit.py's saved `verdicts` are gone. (5) `roll_builtin` rolls a middle-path update only when no scanner ran out of time on its folder (a matching "stuck" entry no longer counts as checked for it) and neither the SkillSpector-and-husk nor the gitleaks stage failed (`MIDDLE_PATH_STAGES`). (6) Each place a merged managed-skills finding names is checked on its own, and the finding rolls only when every place passes; merging also needs both or neither to be a middle-path hold. (7) A full audit that accepts a middle-path update keeps it (`platform_accepted`) for the one info Platform refresh line, which names every file and says "this full audit accepted them". (8) `<bucket>` lines cover only a real UUID pair folder (`_BUCKET_PAIR`). (9) `tests/test_item8_provenance.py`'s engine-swap test is on a new skill folder again and checks the swap happened. README, THREAT_MODEL.md and docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md ("b8i4", D8 corrected) say so. Tests: `tests/test_b8i4_fix_round1.py`, 16 tests (13 replays, one or more per finding, each failing on f9c3d72 at its assertion, and 3 guards). Existing assertions back to f7c28e6's form: `test_item8_release_only.py` H2 (a first run's new folder is a counted high, daily isn't NO_CHANGES, wiped state too), `test_batch5_platform_normalize.py::test_a_skill_file_keeps_its_wording` (reads the counted high's fix), `test_batch6_platform_verify.py::test_content_changes_stay_counted` ("a body that differs from its SKILL.md" is counted) and `::test_a_hit_in_a_file_with_an_unlisted_unit_is_counted` (the skill file's counted WT-I001: gitleaks's hit on it holds it); changed for the reversed rule: the engine-swap test's last step (the planted bytes scanned honestly are a clean new folder in an update run: the quiet line). Fix round 2 (Codex verify of 47d3aba, 4 high and 1 medium, all fixed): (1) `wt.py baseline` saved no findings, so a new skill folder its first baseline (or the owner's yes after a reset) held was a quiet "new-skill" candidate on the next run; every baseline and every run now keep the paths held outside the middle path in `state/platform_held.json` (`save_platform_held`; `platform_held_before` reads it beside `last_findings.json`), a path held before kept while it exists, so a run with other roots doesn't forget it. (2) Each candidate is bound to the real path it had when it was chosen (`PLATFORM_BOUND`), and `platform_still_bound` checks that binding, no link and a platform folder as the home folder has it, before and after `_candidate_why` reads it and again in `roll_builtin`, so a platform folder moved out and replaced by a link while the scanners ran is a counted high ("It moved out of the platform's own folders while Watchtower was checking it.") and keeps its old baseline bytes (overstated: a move found only by the check in `roll_builtin` kept the update out of the baseline but left it the quiet refresh line, with no counted high; fixed in fix round 3). (3) `_instruction_texts` reads every decoded string value of a metadata JSON file, with or without a blank, each once. (4) gitleaks's grouped WT-S002 (its `where` a folder) keeps its files in `affected_files`; `flag_index`/`finding_files` hold a candidate for each file a finding stands for (`affected_files`, `also_flagged_by`), by path and real path, at the severity a carried line was found at (`severity_was`), in settlement and in daily's `hold_on_kept_votes`, and `roll_builtin` holds every affected file (overstated: only lines saved with `affected_files`; a grouped line saved before, carried by a daily or a failed-gitleaks audit, still held nothing; fixed in fix round 3). (5) Daily never early-returned NO_CHANGES or SCANNERS_MISSING while a WT-X005 was open (undone in fix round 3, the owner's decision of Oct 23). README, THREAT_MODEL.md and docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md ("b8i4") say what the first-run and real-path checks cover now. Tests: `tests/test_b8i4_fix_round2.py`, 15 tests (8 replays, each failing on 47d3aba's sources at its assertion, and 7 guards). `tests/test_item8_fix_round3.py`'s two emptied-baseline plants are `md("helper")` again (f7c28e6's form): a new skill folder there on a first baseline stays a counted high under the middle path. Fix round 3 (Codex verify 2 of d2d24e5, 2 high, the final round): (1) a quiet update whose real-path binding failed after settlement stayed the info Platform refresh line. Every run now checks each quiet update again (`hold_unbound_updates`, after settlement and daily's carried lines, before the roll's gate and `summarize_refresh`), and `roll_builtin` checks it once more before the `builtin()` gate, right before it writes the baseline; a failure is a counted high for every place it names (`also_at` included, folded again as a mirror pair), its fix starting "It moved out of the platform's own folders", no refresh entry, and the old baseline hash (`hold_moved_updates`). (2) A grouped result that doesn't name all its files (`coverage_gaps`: a finding on a folder without `affected_files`, as saved before fix round 2, `severity_was` included, or a root-cause group whose `also_count` exceeds its `also_flagged_by` list) holds every candidate under its folder (its skill folder for a root-cause group) in settlement and in daily's `hold_on_kept_votes`, until a scan that finishes names the files; told by what the finding holds and what is on disk, never by a name. (3) Daily on a box with a missing scanner is f7c28e6's again (the owner's decision of Oct 23): fix round 2's WT-X005 check before the SCANNERS_MISSING and NO_CHANGES answers is gone, so those boxes print what they did before b8i4 when nothing else changed (`scanner_alerts` is filled only in a full answer). (4) README, THREAT_MODEL.md and docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md no longer overstate fix round 2's real-path recheck and grouped coverage, and no longer say daily keeps scanner alerts. Tests: `tests/test_b8i4_fix_round3.py`, 9 tests (7 replays, each failing on d2d24e5's sources at its assertion, and 2 guards); `tests/test_b8i4_fix_round2.py`'s scanner-alert test asserts f7c28e6's SCANNERS_MISSING line now.
- b8i4 F1 (blocker, Claude review Oct 23), synced Claude plugins: `.claude/plugins/synced/<bucket>/` holds every plugin synced to the account, the ones the owner installs from marketplaces included, but the release list names only cowork-plugin-management there. On dev a clean new plugin folder there was a "new-skill" candidate (daily said NO_CHANGES, the audit put it in the Platform refresh line as Claude's and rolled it into the baseline), and a path only the baseline had updated quietly. Under `.claude/plugins/synced` only (new `synced_plugin_path`: the path as written, under any home base), `platform_update_candidates` now makes a file a candidate only as an update of a path the release list names (`platform_lock_path`: an exact line or a `<bucket>` line); no "new-skill", "in-skill" or baseline-only "update" candidates there, so everything else there is the counted high it was in 0.7.3. Other platform folders (.grok/bundled, Codex's .system, the managed-skills folders) are unchanged. docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md "b8i4" has the rule. Tests: `tests/test_b8i4_synced_plugins.py`, 6 tests: R a new marketplace plugin is counted (daily not quiet, held, not in the baseline, not in the refresh line); R its update with a new file after two audits holds both; R a marketplace plugin with a baseline line (written as an older run would have rolled it) updated cleanly is a counted high, out of the refresh line, and the baseline keeps its old bytes; G a clean update of a `<bucket>`-listed cowork-plugin-management file in two buckets is the quiet line and rolls in; G a listed managed skill at sand-data and agent-data updates quietly; G an unlisted new managed skill is quiet, rolls in, and its baseline-only update is quiet. The three R tests fail on 4872a9b's watchtower/ at an assertion; the guards pass there and here. No existing assertion changed.
- scripts/scanners.lock: certifi 2026.7.22 -> 2026.10.19 (both hashes, wheel and sdist, checked against PyPI on Oct 23). docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md, doc-only: the prepublish folder-name gap (owner decision Oct 23, 1:27 PM ET) and the folder link into `.claude/plugins/synced` (owner decision Oct 23, 2:24 PM ET) are known limits.
- DQ-048 replay (test-only, Oct 23): `tests/test_dq048_platform_update.py`, the Oct 23 vendor update that dropped an installed v0.7.3's daily score 85 -> 21 (62 high `platform_unverified` files: managed skills under sand-data/ and agent-data/, `.grok/bundled` learn and manifest, Codex's `.system` openai-docs, plus the cursor-public catalog move) replayed through the b8i4 middle path: no counted high, no score drop, one info Platform refresh line naming the files. No code or existing assertion changed.

- b8i1, TruffleHog's launch (with fix rounds 1 and 2): a 0.7.3 regression. Since 0.7.3 every scanner ran from a sealed in-memory copy (`/proc/self/fd/N`), and TruffleHog 3.97.9 runs overseer by default, whose master looks up its own program file, gets `/memfd:watchtower-scanner (deleted)` and exits ("failed to stat binary") before scanning anything. The stage failed and the last key status was kept, but `scanner_used()` only checked the program file, so `engines_used.json` and the `score_history.csv` fingerprint still said TruffleHog ran. (1) `trufflehog_status` (fixplan.py) now passes `--local-dev` after `--no-update` (both kept): an undocumented "hidden" flag in upstream 3.97.9's main.go with which main() runs the scan itself and exits, so overseer never starts. TruffleHog runs from the sealed memfd copy again, like every other scanner; the on-disk private copy that 4c89284 introduced (`DISK_COPY`, `_DISK_COPIES`, `_copy_state`, `_DiskCopy`, `_disk_copy`, `_refuse`'s `said`, the copy branch in `scanner_launch`, and `run()`'s copy checks, own session and process-group kill, with `signal` dropped from wt.py's imports) is removed, so `run()` and `scanner_launch` are as in 1a4805c and `run()`'s time-out is its plain `p.kill()` again (covered by the existing `test_boxes.py::ScannersMisbehave::test_every_scanner_hangs`). (2) The stage counts as finished only on exit 0 or 183 together with TruffleHog's "finished scanning" log line on stderr (new `_trufflehog_finished`: a stderr line that is a JSON object with that `msg`); a silent exit 0 (its signal handler exits 0 on SIGINT/SIGTERM without finishing) and a non-zero exit with partial output are now failed stages ("TruffleHog FAILED (…)", the note "it stopped without saying it finished scanning" for exit 0), so the last key status is kept and TruffleHog isn't an engine used. (3) `scanner_used()` is False for gitleaks, pip-audit, OSV-Scanner and TruffleHog when their audit stage failed (`SCANNER_STAGES`), so `engines_used`, the fingerprint and SCANNERS MISSING follow; SkillSpector and husk are left out (their shared stage also fails on a budget-short run). Unchanged from 4c89284. docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md "b8i1" is rewritten: the flag, read in upstream source and not tried against the real binary offline; the finished rule; a new pin must re-check both. Tests: `tests/test_batch8_trufflehog_launch.py`, 7 tests (T1-T4 rewritten, T5 and T6 unchanged, T7 new): T1 an overseer-like stand-in (fails as overseer does without `--local-dev`) gives its result; T2 TruffleHog's program file is `/proc/self/fd/N` linking to a memfd, a program renamed over `scanners/bin/trufflehog` after the check never runs and nothing is written to the temp folder; T3 its argv has `filesystem`, `--local-dev`, `--no-update`, `--json`; T4 (4 subtests) exit 0 with nothing said, a result with exit 0 and no finished line, a result with an error line and exit 1, a fatal line with exit 1 are each a failed stage, not an engine used nor in the fingerprint, with a "TruffleHog FAILED" note and the last key status kept; T5 and T6 as before; T7 a tripwire on the 3.97.9 pin in scripts/scanner-bins.lock and install.sh. Replayed with this file in place: on 4c89284's watchtower/ 5 failures, 0 errors (T2 at its program-file assertion, T3 at `--local-dev`, T4 (a)-(c) at the skipped stage; T1, T4 (d), T5, T6 and T7 pass); on 1a4805c's watchtower/ (watchtower/ edited back until `git diff --stat 1a4805c -- watchtower` was empty) 11 failures, 0 errors (T1 at its status, `{}` with a "failed to stat binary" note; T3; T4 (a)-(c) at the skipped stage and (d) at engines used; T5; T6's 4 subtests; T2 and T7 pass). Replaced from 4c89284: its T1 (a program that reruns its own path) by T1 here; its T2 (a copy swapped before exec) by T2; its T3 (a copy swapped during the run) and T4 (the private folder is removed) dropped, since no copy or folder is made any more. 8 TruffleHog stand-ins that model a finished scan now also print the finished line (fixtures only): `test_boxes.py` `SCANNERS["trufflehog"]` and the playbook stand-in, `test_dq022_025.py` and `test_dq028_030.py` `SCANNERS`, `test_grok_cli_signin_oct14.py`, `test_batch6_platform_verify.py`, `test_watchtower.py::test_live_dead_keys_drive_severity_and_links`, and `test_rfixes.py`'s mocked `run`; `test_boxes.py` gains `TH_DONE`. No existing assertion changed. Fix round 2 (Codex round 2 on 23476f2): TruffleHog 3.97.9 logs errors it scanned past ("encountered errors during scan", a file it couldn't read), still logs "finished scanning" and exits 0 unless given `--fail-on-scan-errors`, so a file turning unreadable could replace its keys' status with nothing while TruffleHog stayed an engine used; `trufflehog_status` now passes `--fail-on-scan-errors` (with `--local-dev`, `--no-update`, `--json`) and, in case the flag didn't take, also fails the stage on that log line whatever the exit code (`_trufflehog_finished` becomes `_trufflehog_logged`, the set of logged `msg` values; the note says "it hit errors while scanning", never the errors themselves), so a box with a file in scope that is never readable reports TruffleHog FAILED on every run (intended, fail closed: docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md "b8i1"). `run()` (collect.py) starts every program with `start_new_session=True` and, on its time-out or any exception while waiting (Ctrl-C), kills its process group with `os.killpg(p.pid, SIGKILL)` before reaping it (`p.kill()` if that fails), so a helper a scanner started (a detector's `git ls-remote`, run from PATH: the memfd covers TruffleHog's own program only, KNOWN-LIMITS now says) doesn't outlive it; `signal` is back in wt.py's imports, and no `run()` caller needs a terminal. Tests, 3 new and 4 new T4 subtests: T3 also asserts `--fail-on-scan-errors`; T4 (e) a result with the finished line and exit 1, (g) the finished text inside another JSON field and on a plain line, (h) exit 0 with the finished line and a scan-errors line, each a failed stage; T8 exit 183 with a result and the finished line is a finished stage (key status saved, TruffleHog used); T9 the scan-errors note, without the error text; T10 a stand-in that starts `sleep 300 &` and waits: after `run()`'s 2-second time-out the child is gone. On 23476f2 T3, T4 (h), T9 and T10 fail at their assertions; T4 (e), (g) and T8 pass there too (contrasts). Codex round 3 on 63714e4 (no high) found three mediums in `run()`'s own session and process-group kill, accepted as known limits (owner decision 2026-10-22; docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md "b8i1", docs only, no code change): Ctrl-C no longer promptly stops SkillSpector and husk in their worker threads; no `run()` command has a controlling terminal (correction to the above: `cargo update` with `net.git-fetch-with-cli` and a terminal login helper needs one); and `os.killpg` can still run after `communicate()` has reaped the child.
- b8i3, credential-read filter (WT-T004), content only: `cat` counts only a file named exactly `.env` (read, or redirected into); a "Do not … such as `a`, `b`, or `c`" sentence is read as negated (lowered to medium, still listed); `printenv` of one named variable that isn't secret-shaped is not a credential read. A skill whose only hits were these lines no longer gets WT-X003. (rules/text_rules.json: the `cat` alternative of WT-T004's pattern; text.py: new `one_plain_variable` (`PRINTENV_ONE`, `SECRET_SHAPED`) applied in `scan_text` before the reference-file branch, new `_negated_example_list` (`LIST_MODIFIERS`, `EXAMPLE_LIST`, `NEG_HEAD`, `CONDITION`) used by `in_warning` for WT-T004 only.) Every input that still matches keeps its evidence, so keys and dismissals are unchanged. The alarms given up are open owner decisions in docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md "b8i3". Tests: `tests/test_batch8_content_filters.py`, 12 tests; with only text.py and rules/text_rules.json stashed (63714e4's), the 5 replays fail at their assertions and the 7 controls pass. No existing assertion changed. Fix round 1 (Codex review of 0223735: quoted options, a backtick glued to the name, exponential and quadratic backtracking, lost `.env` reads, changed evidence, an affirmative "such as" list lowered): rules/text_rules.json is 63714e4's again (`\bcat\s+\S*\.env\b`), so every input that still matches has 63714e4's evidence, key and dismissal (`cat .env>copy.env` keeps key 7eb4541594f17ae2), and "exactly `.env`" is a content check, new `exact_env_read` (`EXACT_ENV`, `CAT_MATCH`, `LINE_JOIN`; text.py, applied in `scan_text` after `one_plain_variable`): a `cat` match counts only when a word of its command (mask.py's `_LW` shell reading from the match to an unquoted `|`, `;`, `&` or line break, joined lines joined) holds `.env` standing alone, so `.env\`+line break, `${X:-.env}`, `>.env` and `>>.env` flag again; one pass over at most ARGS_CAP characters, a longer command kept. `PRINTENV_ONE` is replaced by `PRINTENV_NAME` and `PRINTENV_REDIRECT` with a linear scan in `one_plain_variable`: the name, bare or quoted, is `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*` (a quoted one may end in one `(x86)`), only blanks, plain redirects and the command's end may follow, and a glued backtick ends it only when it closes one opened before `printenv` on its line. `_negated_example_list` also needs no comma or clause joiner (new `CLAUSE_JOINERS`) between the negation and "such". KNOWN-LIMITS "b8i3" rewritten to match. Tests: 13 more (25 in the file); on 0223735's tree the 7 fix-round replays (one per finding) fail at their assertions, 5 controls and the 12 earlier tests pass; added after that run: one more input in the negation replay and `test_doubt_keeps_it`. The two timing tests (Codex's inputs, 40,000-character lines) allow 1 s each; 0223735 took 5.6 s and 2.4 s. Fix round 2 (Codex review of f61a612: other reads hidden in a `cat` line, dynamic operands, a literal or escaped backtick before `printenv`, a `\r` ending its argument, affirmative "Do not forget to run … such as" lists; every exemption now fails closed, with no shell reading): `exact_env_read` (`EXACT_ENV`, `CAT_MATCH`, `LINE_JOIN`, mask.py's `_LW`) is replaced by `env_variant_cat` (`CAT_PLAIN`, `CAT_REDIRECT`, `CAT_COMMENT`, `_env_variant`): a `cat` match is dropped only when its whole line is lowercase `cat` opening the line, plain operands (`[A-Za-z0-9_./~-]+`) that are all env-variant names (basename starting `.env.` or ending `.env`, not `.env`, not ending in `.`), at most one plain `>`/`>>` redirect whose target's basename isn't `.env`, and an optional `# comment`; any other character, `\r` on the line, a joined line or a line over LINE_CAP keeps it. `one_plain_variable` exempts only lowercase `printenv` (a) opening its line (new `_opens_line`, `_line_bounds`) with only blank-separated plain redirects (`PRINTENV_REDIRECT` narrowed to `[A-Za-z0-9_./-]` targets, plus `2>&1`), then an optional `|| true` (`PRINTENV_TRUE`), then an optional comment after it, or (b) as the line's first code span `` `printenv NAME` ``; a `\r` on the line keeps it. `NEG_HEAD` needs one of 16 action verbs right after the negation. The pattern, evidence and keys are unchanged. Finding 6 (a newly guarded record's key changes) is not redesigned: `guard_record` rehashes every guarded record on purpose (B7), recorded in KNOWN-LIMITS "b8i3". Tests: 18 more (43 in the file), each new loop a subtest per input. Approved change of 6 inputs in 2 fix-round-1 controls (`FixRound1Cat.test_named_env_files_pass`: `cat .env-prod`, `cat a.env.local | grep .env.x`, `cat .env.local; echo .env`; `FixRound1Printenv.test_plain_redirects_still_exempt`: `printenv HOME >>log 2>/dev/null; echo`, `$(printenv HOME)`, `` `printenv 'HOME' >x` ``) from no finding to high, 63714e4's result. On f61a612's code those 2 tests and the 10 fix-round-2 replays fail at their assertions (Codex 1-5: the 10 inputs from Codex's findings; plus `xargs`, a joined line, `CAT`/`PRINTENV`, a later code span, `\r`, a second redirect, after `|| true`, and `printenv HOME 2>&1`, newly exempt); the 8 fix-round-2 controls pass, including a pin of the guarded key (774c69631c16284e becomes 311e713515613b16). Fix round 3 (Codex review of 2b8920c; owner-approved, subtractive, fail loud): `one_plain_variable`'s code-span shape (b) needs no backtick anywhere before it in the text, not just on its line, so a backtick that may close a substitution opened on an earlier line (`` `true `` then `` `printenv AWS_ACCESS_`printf KEY_ID` ``) stays high, as do code spans after any earlier backtick in a file; `_negated_example_list` gives no guard when without, unless, until, before, first or except (new `REQUIRING`) stands between the negation and the match ("Do not run the task without first using commands such as `env`, or `printenv`." stays high); `_line_bounds` returns None for a line over LINE_CAP (4,000), so the documented cap is enforced for `cat` and `printenv`. KNOWN-LIMITS "b8i3" updated: the cap, the code-span and negation rules, and two accepted known limits (owner decision): URL/DSN-style `printenv` names (`DATABASE_URL`, `*_DSN`) are exempt, and dismissals made before b8i3 don't carry over to newly guarded lines (`guard_record` rehash, B7). Tests: 3 more (46 in the file), `FixRound3`; with the three functional lines of text.py edited back to 2b8920c's, all 8 replay inputs fail at their assertions (3 backtick, 3 negation, 2 over-the-cap lines) and the 3 controls pass. No existing assertion changed. Codex verify of 31873e1 (2 high): the affirmative "without—at minimum—using" and "prior to checking" lists are still lowered to medium, an accepted known limit (owner decision, 2026-10-22; DQ-053, KNOWN-LIMITS "b8i3"). DQ-054 is partly fixed and still open, not an accepted limit (the owner called it a regression from b8i3): `SECRET_SHAPED` gains `pwd`, so the `pwd`/`passwd` names `printenv MYSQL_PWD`, `printenv PGPWD` and `printenv pwd` are WT-T004 high again with 63714e4's evidence, key and WT-X003 effect (`DB_PASSWD` stays covered by `pass`; the shell's `PWD` and `OLDPWD` are high again too, as on 63714e4). Not fixed (Codex verify of 7af37e1): `PW`-style names (`PW`, `pw`, `DB_PW`, `MYSQL_PW`, bare, quoted, redirected or with `|| true`) still give no WT-T004 finding and no WT-X003 agreement, where 63714e4 reported them high; this regression from 63714e4 stays open, tracked as DQ-054 (KNOWN-LIMITS "b8i3"). Planned root fix for the next batch: invert the exemption to fail closed, so only a short list of known-harmless names (`PATH`, `HOME`, `USER`, `SHELL`, `LANG`, `TERM` and similar) is exempt and every other single `printenv` name stays high. Tests: 3 more (49 in the file), `Dq054PasswordNames` and `Dq054Agreement`; on 12a9c5c the 2 replays fail at their assertions (3 of the 4 names, `DB_PASSWD` already high, and the missing WT-X003) and the control (`HOME`, `PATH`, `LANG`, `ProgramFiles(x86)` stay exempt) passes. No existing assertion changed.
- b8i5, generic release: fixtures, comments and docs carry no owner, Bot or box names and no real reply host (`reply-hook.example.invalid`); the v0.7.1 owner-calls tests are `tests/test_v071_owner_calls.py`; the platform-yes refusal states the policy without a name; `routines/descriptions.json` holds each routine's name and description (weekly tidy: a preview); `tests/test_generic_release.py` fails on any denylisted string (sha256 only). Item-4 files are exempt until item 4 lands. Fix round 1 (Codex round 1 on 7f2a892, 5 medium and 2 low, all fixed): (1) keyed entries are the digest of the string casefolded with only `[a-z0-9]` kept, so they match in any case; 5 of the 18 are keyed now, and 13 stay exact-form (12 whose text is in no tracked file, so no casefolded digest could be derived; one kept on purpose because its casefolded form is a common word that the lookalike test keeps clean; owner decision pending, docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md "b8i5"); (2) adjacent words are joined into runs of up to 48 characters across any separator or line break, and each text is also read with escapes read and adjacent string literals on a line joined (one linear pass); (3) every path component is scanned; (4) the file list comes from `git ls-files` only, linked worktrees included, and with no git or no checkout the test skips with that reason; (5) the failure text is path, line and digest prefix, with a denylisted path component masked to `[digest prefix]`, and the exact-descriptions check (`same_safely`) scans both values before its `assertEqual`; (6) NUL bytes no longer exempt a file: text is UTF-16 with a BOM, else UTF-8, else latin-1, and only the formats in `BINARY` (the test lists them) are skipped; (7) the daily routine's description (and README's daily-watch row) no longer promises silence: it reports what changed and anything still needing attention (open unverified findings, checks that didn't complete). `ALLOW` (owner decision) holds the digest of the public repo account name exactly as written in the release URLs; only that whole compound is blanked, and any other spelling still hits. Leftovers the stricter test found, replaced with generic stand-ins of the same coverage: `tests/test_hardening_batch4.py:59`, `tests/test_item8_dryrun.py:31`, `tests/test_precision_gate.py:21,25` (`OWNER_KEEPS`), `tests/test_quiet_false_alarms.py:36` (`test_owner_calls_of_oct_10`), `tests/test_dq011_husk_sql.py:31` (a fixture description whose words joined into a skill name). Tests: 12 more (23 in the file): one replay per finding in `FixRound1` (two each for findings 4 and 5, the description one in `RoutineDescriptions`), plus `test_only_the_exact_account_name_is_allowed`, `test_keyed_exact_and_allow_sets` and `test_binary_formats_skipped_are_these`; with 7f2a892's scanner block edited back in (plus three one-line adapters exposing its digest form, failure text and description check under the new names) and its daily description, all 9 replays fail at their assertions. Approved assertion change: `test_matcher_misses_lookalikes` lost its three case-variant lines (the lowercase word, the lowercase word before `-aisec`, the uppercase word before `_BUCKET`), which are must-hit lines in `test_1_case_variants_of_a_keyed_name_hit` now; it injects keyed digests and gained two lookalikes. `test_matcher_finds_every_shape`'s NUL fixture is now a `.png` (a skipped format), so its "skipped" assertion holds unchanged.

## 0.7.3

- Batch 7c fix round (Codex round 1 on 52597c5, one high and one medium; code in plugins.py only; VERSION stays 0.7.2). (1, high, the open "Must-fix before release" item) Script text in command position was not reported as unread: `timeout 30 npx -y npm@10.9.2 exec --package=npm@10.9.2 -- 'npx -y mutable'` (the last word one string, which npm exec hands to a shell as written), the same behind `nice` and inside a pinned outer `npm exec`, gave no WT-M001 (a3e535e reported it as unread), and `x;/usr/bin/npx -y pkg@1.2.3` read as a pinned npx. New `_script_text(word)`: whitespace, a quote or other shell syntax (`_SCRIPT_SYNTAX`: whitespace, `'`, `"`, backtick, `;`, `&`, `|`, `$`, `<`, `>`, `(`, `)`, `{`, `}`, `[`, `]`, `*`, `?`, `!`, `#`, `%`, `^`), or a backslash whose escape names a launcher, shell or wrapper (`_COMMAND_NAMES`; `n\px`, while `C:\tools\npx.cmd` stays a Windows path). `_unwrap(w, i, text)` checks each wrapper's name and the command after them, `mcp_package(…, text)` a package's own command, and `mcp_launches` adds, for the first such word at each launch level, "couldn't read the launch at '<word>'" labelled with the launch that runs it (`npm exec` for the Codex example) or `(command)` at the top, in front of what that level read before. What 52597c5 read is unchanged, so no WT-M001 is lost (`$HOME/bin/npx -y mutable` in a pinned `npm exec` gives the unread line and `npx mutable (no version)`), and a plainly pinned single-word launch stays clear. Fail closed, so there are new false alarms (docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md "Batch 7c", Stricter): a command path built from a variable (`${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/bin/server`, `$HOME/.local/bin/npx`), `%APPDATA%\npm\npx.cmd`, a path with `#`, `!`, `^`, brackets or parentheses, a path with a space after `uvx --from tool==1.0.0`, and `n\px`. (2, medium) Coverage claims: every timeout and nice form the batch 7c entry above names now has a test, and so do the four KNOWN-LIMITS called untested (`nice -n5`, `nice --adjustment 5`, `timeout --signal=TERM`, `timeout -v`): `OptionForms` reads 26 forms, each naming the package in front of `npx -y mutable` and clear in front of `npx -y pkg@1.2.3` (timeout `-v`, `--verbose`, `--foreground`, `--preserve-status`, `-s KILL`, `-s 9`, `-sTERM`, `-s9`, `--signal TERM`, `--signal=TERM`, `--signal=SIGHUP`, `--signal=9`, `-k 5`, `-k5s`, `--kill-after 5`, `--kill-after=5`, durations `0.5`, `2h`, `1d`; nice with no option, `-n 5`, `-n5`, `-n -5`, `--adjustment 5`, `--adjustment=5`, `-10`). No claim was trimmed, since the code supports every form. (Correction to the merge entry above: those four forms are no longer untested.) docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md: the item left "Must-fix before release" (the heading no longer names batch 7c Codex round 1, and the pointer says the batch 7c fix round fixed it). The "Batch 7c" section records the fix and exactly what its tests cover, lists the option forms tested and those the code reads without a test (env's `-0`, `--null`, `-v`, `--debug`, …; timeout's other signals; nice's other signed values), and adds the false alarms. Tests: `tests/test_batch7c_fix2.py`, 9 tests. 7 replays (93 subtests, each failing on 83fd9bf at its assertion, measured with 83fd9bf's watchtower/ and MANIFEST.sha256 stashed, this file in place): `CodexExample` `test_behind_timeout`, `test_behind_nice` (4), `test_inside_a_pinned_outer_npm_exec` (5), `test_at_the_top` (6); `EverySyntax` `test_each_character_in_command_position` (24 characters × 3 positions) and `test_a_backslash_that_hides_a_name` (3); `NothingLost.test_what_83fd9bf_read_is_still_reported` (2). 2 guards (pass on 83fd9bf too): `StaysClean.test_plainly_pinned_single_word_launches_stay_clean` (13) and `OptionForms.test_each_form_is_read` (26). No existing assertion changed. README's "more than 1600 tests" still holds.
- Batch 7d (review of the batch 7c fix round, three items; code in plugins.py only; VERSION stays 0.7.2). (1) An empty command word stopped the reading: `timeout 30 npx -y npm@10.9.2 exec --package=npm@10.9.2 -- "" -c "npx -y mutable"` gave no WT-M001, though npm runs its shell in place of an empty command; the same behind `nice` and inside a pinned outer `npm exec`. `_script_text` now calls a blank word (empty or whitespace only) script text, and `mcp_package` goes on past an empty word after a launcher's options or `--` (it stopped when that word was `""`), so each of those gives `npm exec (couldn't read the launch at '')` and `npm exec` at `'npx -y mutable'`; a blank top-level `"command"` is kept as one word (`_mcp_servers`), and `mcp_launches` labels a launch with a blank program name with the launch that runs it, not with an empty label. (2) `_mcp_servers` split a top-level `"command"` string at whitespace, erasing a line continuation: a plugin hook's `true` / `n\` / `px -y mutable` on three lines read as `n\` and `px`. The string is now kept whole, and so is script text (`(command)`), when it is blank or when whitespace follows a backslash, a `^` or a backtick (`_SPLIT_ERASES`); other strings split as before. (3) The fix round made `"command": "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/bin/server"` a WT-M001, though the host fills in `${VAR}` placeholders before launch. `_script_text` reads one leading `${NAME}` (any name matching `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*`) followed by `/` or `\` and a plain path as that path (`_HOST_PLACEHOLDER`), checking the rest as any command word and still reading its last path part as the program (`${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/bin/npx -y mutable` stays `npx mutable (no version)`, a placeholder path to a shell stays unread); bare `$HOME`, `$(…)`, `${A}${B}`, `${A:-x}`, a placeholder mid-word, `${A}` alone and `${A}/` stay "couldn't read". Where npx or npm exec hand the word to a shell (the first command word they run), `mcp_launches` keeps a placeholder as script text, since the shell reads what it is filled in with. Tests: `tests/test_batch7d.py`, 9 tests. 5 replays (26 subtests, each failing on the staged fix-round state at its assertion, measured with that state's watchtower/ and MANIFEST.sha256 in place, this file in place): `EmptyCommand` `test_codex_example_with_an_empty_command` (4, staged: `[]`) and `test_an_empty_command_after_wrappers_and_at_the_top` (6, staged: `[]`); `LineContinuation` `test_a_continued_line_at_the_top` (6, staged: `[]`) and `test_a_continued_line_in_a_plugin_hook` (staged: `[]`); `PlaceholderPath.test_a_host_placeholder_path_is_a_path` (9, staged: one "couldn't read the launch" line each). 4 guards (pass on the staged state too): `LineContinuation.test_plain_command_strings_split_as_before` (5), `PlaceholderPath.test_its_last_component_is_still_read` (3), `test_other_dollar_forms_stay_unread` (21) and `test_a_placeholder_a_shell_reads_stays_unread` (2). Removed false alarm: a host placeholder path in command position, except where npx or npm exec hand it to a shell (still one, docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md "Batch 7c", Stricter). New false alarms (same list): a blank command word where nothing would run it, and a top-level command string with a harmless escape before whitespace (`/opt/my\ tools/server`). Open limit recorded, not fixed: `npm exec` with no command at all after `--` gives no WT-M001. docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md: the "Batch 7c" section records items 1-3 as fixed in batch 7d with exactly what `tests/test_batch7d.py` covers; the fix round's script-text item stays fixed. No existing assertion changed. README's "more than 1600 tests" still holds.
- Batch 7d part 2 (the last "Must-fix before release" items; VERSION 0.7.3; MASK_VERSION 10). (A, 7a masking, `mask.py`: new `_json_text`, `armor_blocks`) A key block written with escapes ended at an escaped END whose `\n` may be text: a JS ``String.raw `…` `` with whitespace before the template (or any other tag) slipped past the raw-literal check, and a shell's double-quoted `"…\n"` fragments (no escape processing in sh) next to a comment header's END ended masking, so the body after it was kept. Now such a block has an END only at the first escape level of a text that is one JSON document (`json.loads` of the whole text); anywhere else it hides to the end of the text and is a key. MASK_VERSION 9 -> 10, so copies masked at 9 are masked again (approved copies, the engine cache). (B, 7a classification, `text.py` `_key_shaped`) A body framed as OpenPGP public packets (an RSA modulus, P-256 coordinates, a 32/56/57-byte X25519/X448/Ed25519/Ed448 key, two packets for Ed25519) got the "isn't a real key" note and the example fix-plan group though a private key's bytes fit inside; now any body whose first byte may start an OpenPGP packet is a key (`pgp_packets` still reads them; its "public" gives no note). The finding's rule and severity are unchanged; for such blocks the example id WT-T011x becomes the ordinary WT-T011, which shows in each finding's `rule` (scan_text, the audit's findings and `last_findings.json`), in the fix text (no "This looks like an example or checksum value (a key block that isn't a real key)" note, also on WT-S001/WT-S002), in the fix plan's group ("Keys left in files", not "Keys that look like examples or checksums") and in the gitleaks note counting example-looking hits; RISK_KIND, vet's verdict, the brief, prepublish and corroboration already read WT-T011x as WT-T011, so they don't change. (C, 7b `chat_webhook`, `text.py`) Doubt now also covers: (C1) an opening `<pre`/`<code` tag with anything but whitespace before its `>` (`_HTML_OPEN_ATTR` in `_code_doubt`; a quoted attribute could hold the closing tag and end the code region early); (C2) any redirection in an accepted curl/wget command, an unquoted `<` or `>` in any word or `&>` right after it (`_shell_literal`, `_QUOTED_PART`, `_AMP_REDIRECT`; the redirection skip is gone: `-H >o -d -x…` gave `-H` the next option for the shell); (C3) the word `with` (`_DOUBT_WORDS`; JavaScript's `with` can change what the client name means). A Slack or Discord webhook in such a document is the critical WT-T005 (it was the medium WT-T018). (D, 7b, `rules/text_rules.json`) WT-T018's Office branch is anchored, `(?<![\w-])[\w-]+\.webhook\.office\.com/`: linear on long words (was about 9 s at 64,000 characters); every match the tests name is kept, and only a host glued right after another branch's match (`api.telegram.org/botx.webhook.office.com/`) loses its second match. Tests: `tests/test_batch7d_part2.py`, 19 tests; 10 replays (32 cases), each failing on 006c719 at its assertion (measured with 006c719's watchtower/, rules/ and MANIFEST.sha256 restored, this file in place): `EscapedEnds` `test_string_raw_with_a_space_before_its_template` (5: 38 body pieces kept each), `test_a_shells_double_quoted_fragments` (38 kept), `test_literals_outside_json_give_no_end` (3: hid only to the END); `PublicFraming` `test_public_framing_is_a_key` (6: "example") and `test_through_gitleaks_it_is_a_key_left_in_a_file` (noted); `WebhookDoubt` `test_c1_a_quoted_attribute_in_an_opening_tag` (3), `test_c1_any_attribute_is_doubt`, `test_c2_any_redirection_in_the_command` (8) and `test_c3_with_changes_the_client_name` (3), each WT-T018; `OfficeWebhookPattern.test_long_words_are_read_in_linear_time` (past the child's 60 s limit). 8 guards pass there too (`test_json_documents_still_end`, `test_a_json_fake_text_body_is_still_noted`, `test_real_line_blocks_are_unchanged`, `test_every_t018_match_is_kept`, `test_a_fake_text_body_is_still_noted`, `test_c1_a_bare_tag_stays`, `test_c2_quoted_angle_brackets_stay`, `test_c3_with_inside_a_word_stays`), and one guard is new (`test_string_raw_without_a_space_stays_hidden`: the body was hidden on 006c719 too, but it records masked 9, not 10).
